# Flags

Every engine switch read from the environment, recounted from `src` at `57f50fc3` on 2026-10-22. This includes #210's promotion and #205's two additional default-on switches. Flags that are not on develop (parked, removed from a PR, or only on a side branch) are kept in [PARKED.md](PARKED.md) so the ideas are not lost. Registration rules are in [PLAN.md](PLAN.md): at most 10 experimental flags, and each expires two reviews after it lands.

There are 529 flags read from the environment in `src` (plus the tackle package (run/tackle-repair, off): DIVE_STANCE, TACKLE_REACH, LUNGE_FLIGHT, REACH_TIME, GATHER_ATTACK, HOLD_AIM, FREE_ARM_2D, FREE_ARM_FULL, ONE_ARM_SHARE, ONE_ARM_PULL and REACH_ARRIVAL; 2026-11-25, engine-102: engine-101 plus HANDOFF_FREEZE (on, a bug fix; Coverage); 2026-11-24, the QB quick-game landing: plus QB_V2 (off) and QB_QUICK_DRIFT (a mode, 0); 2026-11-24, engine-101: engine-100 plus BLOCK_FRAME (on; Defense); engine-100: engine-99 plus SCOUT_SEEN (on, leak removal; Flex); engine-99: engine-98 plus QB_SCOUT and QB_SCREEN_SEEN (on, leak removals; QB lane); engine-98: engine-97 plus HANDS_ON_ARMS, SECURE_SPLIT and SECURE_FACE_GEOM (on); recounted 2026-11-24, with CALL_COMPOSE (off, rules/call-compose) counted: BELIEVED_BODY (off, experimental; Flex) on top of engine-97; engine-97: engine-96 plus QB_SCAN and QB_CANON_SEEN (on, leak removals; Flex; recounted from `src`, the on count also takes in engine-96's BALL_DRAG); engine-96: engine-95 plus BALL_DRAG (on); engine-95: engine-94 plus THROWAWAY_FELT (on); engine-94: engine-93 plus FLIGHT_BREAK (on) and FLIGHT_LEAD (off, parked); engine-93: engine-92 plus HEAD_FOLLOW (on); engine-92: engine-91 plus PERIPH_ACUITY (on); engine-91: engine-90 plus STEM_WORK (on); engine-90: engine-89 plus READ_VERTICAL_LOS and BACK_CLEAR (on); engine-89: engine-88 plus FLAT_CARRY (on); engine-88: engine-87 plus FORECAST_FINISH and QB_ROLE_PATH (on), READ_WINDOW and QB_CLOSE_BELIEF (off); engine-87: engine-86 plus HALF_KEY (on); engine-86 was engine-85 plus MUSH_MATCH (on); engine-85 was engine-84 plus FLAT_SINK (on); engine-84 was engine-83 plus DOWN_RISE_PLANT (on); engine-83 was engine-82 plus QB_DRIVE, QB_RHYTHM_THROW, QB_ALIGN, QB_BODY_FOLLOW, QB_WINDOW_ARRIVE, QB_KEY_PLANT, QB_STICK, QB_PEEK_CLOCK, QB_MOF_READ, QB_DEEP_WAIT and QB_STANCE; QB_DRIVE, QB_RHYTHM_THROW, QB_WINDOW_ARRIVE, QB_KEY_PLANT and QB_STICK on): 388 are on unless set to `0`, 104 are off unless set to `1`, and 29 are numeric scales or modes (identity value unless listed in the stack's numeric section). The rows below carry each flag's history.

**Promotion (2026-11-09):** the reference stack `run-ref-4` is now the engine's default. The 90 flags it lists `on` are read as on unless set to `0` (rows marked "promoted" below); the rest are unchanged. Decisions and evidence for each flag are in [REFERENCE_STACK.md](REFERENCE_STACK.md); `npm run ref -- --list` is the current inventory, and `npm run ref -- --off -- <command>` runs the engine as it was before. The test suites still run on the pre-promotion engine (pinned in `vitest.config.ts`) until their recorded cases are re-recorded. The counts in the paragraph above predate all of this.

| `PRO_CALL` | on | promoted 2026-11-11 (engine-28; `=0` restores); was experimental (protection call, 2026-11-11) | protection | `src/sim/behaviors/blocking.ts` (`ProtectionCoordinator`, `boxThreats`), `src/sim/assign.ts` (back install) | the protection is called at the line from what the offence sees before the snap: the box count (defenders within 3.5 yd of the line inside the linemen's width +-3.5 yd: on-line men, walked-up backers, a safety in the box). Walked-up threats are declared with the line, so the snap assignment slides to the extra man and leaves the one the line can't take to a back; a declared threat is a rusher on his first seen downhill step (BLITZ_READ's rule, `docs/reviews/patches/blitz-read.patch`) and is released when seen leaving the box (held up to 1.2 s). When the count exceeds the protectors the concept keeps in, a back installed on a free route stays in as a check-release; `coord.backsIn` is the call's decision for the back-release builder. A blitz disguised from depth is not in the count; a called threat who doesn't come frees his blocker after 0.6 s; short of the count an attached end also checks first. Evidence: docs/reviews/2026-11-11-protection.md. Card (league-sampled, seeds 101-116 + 201-216, unpaired, 2,892 vs 3,092 dropbacks): free first pressure at even / +1 numbers 23.2 / 38.0% -> 13.7 / 20.8% (NFL 12.7 / 14.9), all 12.1 -> 9.6% (NFL 10.2); any pressure 28.4 -> 27.7% (NFL 27.8); 7+ in against 6+ rushers 29.8 -> 37.4% (NFL 42.2); sacks 2.1 -> 1.9%. Truth trace (16 games): free blitzing backers 67 -> 11, free DL 115 -> 140, all free 218 -> 188. Big on big (linemen take the down linemen first; run-ref-25, reverted): free blitzers 62 -> 44, free DL 124 -> 146, even / +1 18.9 / 37.0 -> 22.7 / 35.2%. The extra free DL are rush wins after engagement, not unassigned men: the cost is lost help (an uncovered lineman held on a declared backer doesn't double in time). Not a promotion candidate until the held lineman can help while his backer hasn't come. Attempt 3 (big on big, 2026-11-11): the declaration's matching is type-aware, so leaving a called off-ball threat to the back is cheap (1 + 0.6 dy) and leaving a man on the line to him is not (8 + 0.6 dy); a back on a down lineman can still come from the count or from his own pickup after the snap. Three paired 16-game sets, league-sampled, seeds 101-116 | 201-216 | 301-316, off -> on: free DL on the line 97 -> 120 | 92 -> 103 | 152 -> 165 (per 100 dropbacks +0.97 | +0.46 | +0.84; within +-15 on two of three); free off-ball LB blitzers 59 -> 16 | 58 -> 19 | 51 -> 21; free first pressure at even numbers 21.8 -> 11.8 | 20.0 -> 15.2 | 21.6 -> 19.9%, +1 50.0 -> 18.2 | 42.9 -> 16.7 | 39.3 -> 17.9%; any pressure 27.8 -> 25.8 | 31.6 -> 27.7 | 36.0 -> 35.7%; a back on a down lineman by the call at the snap 12 -> 6 | 16 -> 17 | 13 -> 14 (his own pickups 14 | 14-17 | 10-24); interior DL free untouched (dtfree.py, past the line) sim 0.07-0.14 per 100 either arm vs NFL 0.09. A check-release back whose called backer drops leaves at about 1.3 s. Tests `test/pro-call.test.ts`; diag scripts `scripts/diag/pass-operators/{doubles,unblocked}.mts`, `dtfree.py`. |

A receiver on his route who sees a defender in his lane ahead (his perceived picture, 0.25-0.45 s ahead at his pace by route running) works to a shoulder: toward his next break, else the defender's open shoulder; width radii + 0.15-0.4 yd by route running; he eases 2-6% through the bend; the waypoint and timing are kept; contact is not forbidden (a defender who mirrors can still close). | `SPACING_HOLD` | on | promoted 2026-11-16 (engine-70; `=0` restores); was experimental (carrier spacing, 2026-11-15; `=1` turns on; docs/reviews/2026-11-15-spacing-hold.md) | carriers | `src/sim/spacing.ts` (`holdBend`, `bendHolds`, `limitHolds`, the carrier branch of `gatherForMotion` and the headway loop of `spaceTeammates`) | set at first review. A carrier (or a lead blocker on the carrier's steer) keeps his decision around a teammate: the bend he took stays on its side and may grow smoothly while the held request still conflicts with that peer, and he returns to the request only when it clears by a body width or he has passed the peer; a headway limit he is braking under is kept until the closing line clears the touch distance by a body width (enter at the touch distance, exit a body width beyond). Without it both choices were re-made from scratch every tick on a knife-edge, and the sight flags' aged, stepped pictures exposed it (carrier / spacing 0.122/s at engine-49, 0.170/s at engine-66). Blockers and defenders share the function but not these branches. |
| `DIR_CAP` | on | promoted 2026-11-15 (engine-69; `=0` restores); was experimental (physics, 2026-11-15; `=1` turns on; docs/reviews/2026-11-15-dir-cap.md) | physics | `src/physics/kinematics.ts` (`directionalSpeedFactor`, `trunkPrice`, `effectiveVmax`, the start cap), `src/physics/world.ts` (the push and the leg-drive caps) | set at first review. The speed cap by the angle of the hips off the line of travel is monotone (full to 20 deg, straight down to the backpedal's share at 130 deg, flat beyond): the legacy curve has side-on the slowest at 0.60, below the backpedal at 0.68, where the NFL (BDB 2025, 322 zone DBs, top-decile speed over own p98 by shoulder angle off travel) has 1.00 to 30 deg, 0.91 at 52, 0.81 at 82, 0.75 at 97, 0.62 beyond 135. And the trunk's twist costs 0.0018 per degree (a stated choice checked against the NFL twist price, not a fit): with hips on the line a 22 / 45 / 55 deg twist costs 3 / 7 / 8 percent of top speed. Off: bit for bit the legacy cap. |

| `CARRY_TURN` | on | promoted 2026-11-16 (engine-72; `=0` restores); was experimental (C9 / task 117, CARRY_POSTURE 3c, 2026-11-16; needs `CARRY_POSTURE=1`; `=1` turns on; docs/reviews/2026-11-16-carry-posture-3c.md) | defense (deep carry) | `src/sim/behaviors/defense.ts` (`carryOpenRun`, ZoneCoverage deep over and ManCoverage deep carry after CUSHION_BREAK's turn) | set at first review. CUSHION_BREAK's turn opens him; it does not turn him: with the cushion broken he runs with the climber hips on his line of travel and his look back on the QB within his head's reach (neck plus trunk), paying DIR_CAP's trunk price for the twist. He turns fully (look to his receiver and the ball) on a seen event: the receiver even with or past him in his own picture (signed cushion at or under 0, undone above +0.5 yd) or his seen throw toward his receiver (playsThrow). The full turn holds until both clear. NFL witness (BDB 2025 wk 1-9, nflcarry_bins.py): torso 56-81 deg off the QB until level, 34-45% past 135 deg at cushion 0 to -0.5 with the ball in the air; ours (3b) past 135 deg on 60% at +1.0 yd before any throw. |
| `OFF_PEDAL` | 3 (numeric; default since engine-73, 2026-11-16; `=0` restores); was 0 (numeric bitmask) | experimental (QCORNER bit 1, shared across off-man techniques; 2026-11-16; docs/reviews/2026-11-16-off-pedal.md) | defense (ManCoverage: off-man corners in man calls, the quarters MOD corner, match carriers in zone calls) | `src/sim/behaviors/defense.ts` (ManCoverage pre-release pedal) | bit 1: before the receiver's release is in his picture an off-aligned man gives ground at the slow-play pace (1.5 yd/s; bit 2 → 1.0, bit 4 → 2.0; a stated choice, no existing quantity before the stem), square, bounded by his own backpedal capacity (`pedalRequest`), taking the cushion target if the stem comes faster; the seen release ends it. Before, he held his cushion against a receiver who had not moved and stood (0.5-0.8 yd/s through 1.0 s in man calls; 0.4-0.5 yd/s to 0.75 s for the quarters corner, NFL 0.5 / 1.5 yd/s at 0.5 / 1.0 s). Press and the Cover-3 corner under CORNER_DROP unchanged. Bit 2 (settle on the stop, needs bit 1, so OFF_PEDAL=3): when the stem-stop read says his receiver stopped, the off-man corner stops gaining depth gradually and never comes forward before he has seen the throw; his drive comes on the ball. Quarters corners, stops before the throw, his depth change in the 0.5 s after: -0.7 -> 0.0 yd (NFL +0.5; inside 5 yd +0.2 vs NFL +0.9); vertical cushion unchanged (4.1 / 2.8); hitch comp 0.96 both, corner 4.3 -> 4.9 yd at the throw (docs/reviews/2026-11-16-off-pedal.md). |
| `QCORNER` | 0 (numeric bitmask) | experimental (quarters corner, 2026-11-16; docs/reviews/2026-11-16-off-pedal.md) | defense (quarters MOD corner) | `src/sim/coverage/rules.ts` (`qCorner`), `src/sim/coverage/techniques.ts` (`QuarterShort`) | bit 4: the quarters MOD corner who has zoned off a short #1 plays #1's path from his own picture instead of the deep quarter's drop to its landmark: #1 stays on his side, he settles at his depth over #1's window, outside-in, eyes on the QB; #1 crosses (2+ yd inside his alignment in his picture, final once declared) he sinks and works inside to the window 5 yd over the crosser (stated). His break on the throw is the base technique's (`playsThrow` -> `playBall`). Before, he kept bailing: 1.9 yd of sink from 1.0 to 2.0 s against the NFL's 0.4 (#1 stays) / 1.3 (#1 crosses). Bits 1 and 2 are unused (the pedal is OFF_PEDAL; the alignment bit was dropped). |
| `ROB_COMMIT` | 7 (numeric bits; default since engine-81, 2026-11-18, a coached-rule repair; `=0` restores); was 0 | promoted 2026-11-18 (engine-81; gap 5 of Rules' coverage-position table), landed without a card | defense (a safety helping #1: quarters safety with #2 out/under, palms safety with #2 under, the 3x1 weak safety) | `src/sim/coverage/rules.ts` (`helpOne`, `helpOneCommitted`) | Legacy re-scores the help every tick (over the top while #1 reads vertical or has ever climbed past 7 yd, else rob), so the safety flips rob/top/rob as an under or a dig crosses 7 yd (30 of 98 rob plays in cover4/cover6 had 3+ job changes before the throw) and robs a #1 who has not declared. bit 1: an undeclared #1 leaves him in his quarter. bit 2: commit, help changes only on a new declaration by #1 believed after his last change (vertical → top, in → rob); a past climb alone does not move him. bit 4: a short #1 (under/stop/out/flat, or an in, inside 7 yd) is not his to rob; he plays his quarter. On (=7, 6 seeds all concepts): yards an attempt 8.79 -> 8.81, 10-20 yd YAC 6.52 -> 6.72; Cover 4 YAC 4.99 -> 4.75, Cover 2/3/6 unchanged; rob entries on a short #1 60 -> 13, on an undeclared #1 4 -> 0, safety plays with 3+ job changes before the throw 30 -> 15 (the rest are new declarations by #1). |

Measured (a2-chain, engine-75): shoulder break +0.25 -> +0.10 s after the release; toward the catch point at +0.2 / +0.4 s 0.2 / 1.1 -> 0.6 / 1.4 yd/s (NFL 1.9 / 2.4); staged zone YAC 5.68 -> 5.27, 20+ 5.3 -> 4.7%; short comp 75.5 -> 75.6%, PBU 97 -> 100, short YAC 5.11 -> 4.70. Bit 2 adds little (zone YAC 5.16) and raises short comp to 77.5% with PBU 90. |

| `CARRY_READ` | on | promoted 2026-11-13 (engine-57; `=0` restores); was experimental (C14 coverage, 2026-11-13; built in Defense's CUSHION_BREAK code, Defense reviews) | coverage | `src/sim/behaviors/defense.ts` (`ManCoverage.trackReceiver` protectDepth request floor; `carrySpeedNeed` / `burstNeed` at both call sites), `src/sim/coverage-leverage.ts` (`seenAcceleration`) | set at first review. A deep carry plays off what the receiver can still become, read from his seen upfield speed and the acceleration in the defender's picture, continued as a burst fading over 1.0 s (estimated): (1) while the receiver will still be climbing 0.5 s on (hold weight continuous from 1 to 3 yd/s projected), a man carry with installed deep responsibility asks for at least hold x the upfield speed his carry needs: he spends cushion only as fast as it allows and never comes downhill at a climber; as the receiver throttles the hold fades; (2) the carry need is the slowest speed now that keeps the gap within his spare cushion over 2.0 s (estimated) while the receiver bursts and he accelerates toward his facing's cap until a rounded turn to the run would be done, then toward his top speed. Off: as No. 1 passed 3 yd/s at about 0.8 s the over-the-top roof asked a corner 6-9 yd over him to come downhill at 4-6 yd/s, then the need from vx alone kept him square at a pedal. No. 1 on a go vs Cover 3, ball held (BDB 2025 weeks 1-3 n 37; seeds 11/23 x 8 leagueSample games, unpaired; `scripts/diag/risk/vert-pos.ts`, `vert_sep_nfl.py --pos`), off -> on (NFL): corner speed at 1.5 s about 2 -> 2.5-3.3 yd/s (3.1); over him at 1.5 / 2.0 s 3.3-3.6 / 1.6-2.0 -> 3.8 / 2.4-2.5 (4.9 / 3.3); on top at 2.85 s 17-25% -> 27-40% (54%). No. 1's hitch vs cover3_match, ball forced at the break (`scripts/diag/risk/break-panel.ts`, 32 seeds): over him at the break 3.0 -> 3.5 yd, caught 75 -> 71%, 7.7 -> 8.1 yd a throw (NFL hitch vs Cover 3, `short_cushion_nfl.py`: 3.2 over, 68%, 6.2). | `CATCH_POINT` | on | promoted 2026-11-11 (engine-31; `=0` restores); was experimental (C14 open end, 2026-11-11) | catch | `src/sim/catching.ts` (`resolveCatchOpportunities`) | set at first review. A tracking receiver catches the ball where it comes to his hands, not where it first crosses the edge of his reach: while his closest approach is still ahead (ball closing on him at the end of the tick and in the air), his catch waits for it. Off, open on-target balls were decided at a median 1.38 yd from his centre with the stretch penalty near full: on the panel, nobody within 3 yd, 58% caught (25% failed the hard-catch roll, 11% the drop roll); NFL 81%. | `CATCH_SECURE` | on | promoted 2026-11-12 (engine-39; `=0` restores); was experimental (C14, 2026-11-11; mechanism kept, read waits for defenders who arrive) | catch | `src/sim/catching.ts` (`receiverContest`, `secureStep`), `src/sim/playsim.ts` (`updateBall`) | set at first review; estimated. A won catch roll puts the ball in the receiver's hands for a securing window ((0.22 + 0.3 x difficulty) x (1.25 - 0.5 x hands), panel median 0.29 s) before it is a catch; each defender arriving in it gets one resolution from what he delivers (a hand to the ball, or a hit by closing speed into the body), resisted by the receiver's hands and contested catch and by how far he has got; a dislodged ball deflects (DEFLECT_BALL) and stays live. Panel: 6 dislodges in 244 windows: defenders do not close on the receiver in the window (requested velocity turns off him; body 4.5 vs NFL 6.5 yd/s). Not read in games until they do. Test `test/catch-hands.test.ts`. |
| `HANDS_ON_ARMS` | on | default (physics/catch, Tools 2026-11-24; `=0` restores); a defect fix | catch | `src/sim/catching.ts` (`contestHands`) | review 2: drop the off path. CATCH_HANDS's contest returned `add = 0` always (the accumulator was declared in the first CATCH_HANDS commit f26fc0611 and never written), so the difficulty term `diffHands` was dead and a defender who reached the receiver's arms and lost his rake roll added nothing. Each such defender now adds `depth x technique`, the two terms the rake roll multiplies (no new constant); the receiver's hands and contested-catch skill scale it where `diffHands` is applied. 26 events in 16 games, mean -0.079 on a mean catch chance of .861 |
| `SECURE_SPLIT` | on | default (physics/catch, Tools 2026-11-24; `=0` restores); a defect fix | catch | `src/sim/catching.ts` (`secureStep`) | review 2: drop the off path. | `SECURE_FACE_GEOM` | on | default (physics/catch, Tools 2026-11-24; `=0` restores); a defect fix | catch | `src/sim/catching.ts` (`secureStep`, `facesPoint`) | review 2: drop the off path. | `DEFLECT_BALL` | on | promoted 2026-11-11 (run-ref-22; `=0` restores); was experimental (G3 catch, 2026-11-11) | catch | `src/sim/catching.ts` (`receiverContest` miss path, `deflect`, `defenderPlay` tipped ball) | set at first review. A ball the receiver fails to catch comes off him only if his hands got to it: P(touch) = 1 - 0.7 x stretch^2 - 0.6 x high - 0.5 x low (at least 0.15) from where the ball was in his catch window; untouched it keeps its flight (overthrow picks stay live). Touched squarely, a ball he did not hold pops up and dies (15% of its horizontal speed, vz ~ N(0.1 + 1.6 x solid^2, 0.4)); a glancing touch at the edge of his reach keeps up to 55% and stays low. A defender catches a tipped ball only within his hands' reach and seeing it. Off, every miss popped the ball up at 20% speed beside the receiver, silently, and defenders caught it 10-40% of the time: 17 of 21 short interceptions on the league-sampled panel came off the receiver (3.8% of short throws, NFL 0.4%). League-sampled panel (788 dropbacks), off -> on: INT per 100 throws 5.0 -> 2.5 (0-10 yd 4.8 -> 2.4, NFL 1.1; 10-20 6.2 -> 3.6, NFL 4.9); off-receiver picks 18 -> 5 of the INTs; completion 61.5 -> 60.9%; yards a pass play paired -0.12 +/- 0.09; YAC median 4.56 -> 4.57. 16-game card: INT 3.6 -> 2.1% (NFL band 1.47-3.15), completion 61.0 -> 61.0%. Attempt 1 (every touched ball fast and low, torso window) overshot (card INT 1.2%). The miss event (`miss`) is logged unflagged. Test `test/deflect-ball.test.ts`. |

| `NICKEL_FRONTS` | on | promoted 2026-11-09 (run-ref-4; `=0` restores); was experimental | defense | `src/playbook/defense.ts` (catalog), `src/playbook/alignment.ts` (roles, Mint edge), `src/game/playcaller.ts` (calls), `src/playbook/types.ts` (3-3-5 personnel) | set at first review. Sub-package fronts, named by their coaching families: Penny (5-1: 4i-0-4i with both edges on the line, the Will walked up as the weak edge and rushing on zone calls, the Mike alone off), Penny Bear (5-1 with the 3-0-3 bear interior), Mint (4i-0-4i, one edge to the strength, two off-ball backers with the Will over the weak C-gap bubble), Okie (5-0-5 three down with a stand-up rover who drops on 3rd and 7+ and rushes otherwise), and the 3-3 Stack (new `stack335` personnel: three down, three backers stacked at 4.5 yd; 3% of nickel calls). Shares from 2022 snap frames classified by interior shape (`scripts/diag/align/nfronts.py`, same code on the sim): NFL nickel early bear 6%, tite/mint 3%, 5-1 4%; short yardage bear 12%, 5-1 6%; 3rd and long nose-only 5%; dime nose-only 11% early / 21% on 3rd and long. Called: nickel early Penny 2, Penny Bear 5, Mint 3, Okie 1%; 3rd down 1/3/1/5%; short yardage 3/10/3%; dime Okie 11 / 18 / 12%. Sim with the flag (same classifier): nickel early bear 7%, 5-1 4%, nose-only 5%; short yardage bear 10%; dime 3rd and long nose-only 16%. With the flag a side whose widest man on the line plays an inside technique keeps it (the Mint has no weak edge). The fronts exist only under the flag; their line contacts match Over/Under's. An earlier version (one five-man and one three-man front) ran on the PR-tier scorecard within noise (stuffs 13.7 -> 10.8%) |

| `MUG_PRESSURES` | on | promoted 2026-11-09 (run-ref-4; `=0` restores); was experimental | defense | `src/playbook/defense.ts` (mug_one / mug_sim / mug_fire / mug_both), `src/game/playcaller.ts` (package pick), `src/playbook/call-integrity.ts` (flag) | set at first review. A call that mugs backers in the A gaps (MUG_CALLS or the passing-down mug) also picks a mug package: Mug One (Mike A, Will bails, 5 rush), Mug Sim (Mike A, weak end drops to the hook, 4 rush), Mug Fire (both A, weak end drops as seam/flat, Cover 3, 5 rush), Mug Both (double A, six rush, Cover 0); the remainder bails (both muggers drop, 4 rush). Shares from 2022 nickel dropbacks with a mug at the snap (`scripts/diag/align/mugrush.py`, `wasInitialPassRusher`): double mug (n 173) no mugger rushes 42%, one 32%, both 25%, a lineman drops 14%, rush 4/5/6+ 49/24/22%; single mug (n 341) the mugger rushes 50%. Sim, 40 games with MUG_CALLS=1 (`scripts/diag/_mugpkg.mts`): nickel double mug (n 44) no mugger rushes 34%, one 31%, both 18%, a lineman drops 4%, rush 4/5/6 54/30/16%; single mug (n 85) the mugger rushes 46%. Limit: the protection (`ProtectionCoordinator.isRusher`) reads each defender's private rush/blitz assignment, so a bail or sim cannot yet leave a free rusher or waste a blocker; the package mix is right but its effect on protection is not yet real. Tests `test/mug-pressures.test.ts`. |
| `BACKER_TAKEON` | on | promoted 2026-11-09 (run-ref-4; `=0` restores); was experimental | defense | `src/sim/behaviors/defense.ts` (`takeOn`, applied to backers' run fits) | set at first review. An unblocked backer who sees a climber coming at him breaks down before contact: he brakes his speed into the blocker to 0.5 yd/s and sinks his pads to the engaged run-fit level, keeping his fit's facing. A climber, from the backer's own picture: a blocker off his block, moving at him at 1.5 yd/s or more, within 2.5 yd, closing at 2 yd/s or more, whose closest approach comes inside his arms (0.45 yd), with contact due within the time the backer's brakes need plus one beat. He doesn't break down when the carrier he sees is closer than the blocker. Why: on Coverage's 22 explosive Cover 1 / 2-Man runs (#419 `efe11322`), a climbing TE or H-back's first collision knocked a backer down before the carrier reached the line on 7 snaps (`scripts/diag/_cfit.mts`, `_tefall.mts`); Flex confirmed the collision physics is coherent and named breaking down and hands as the physical levers. Result, 400 matched random run snaps (seeds 5000-5399; `scripts/diag/_takeonpair.mts`, `_takeonep.mts`): it fires on 67% of run snaps; the 133 where it doesn't fire are identical. Backer falls in the first 3 s 114 → 56. Paired yards on fired snaps -1.07 (95% CI -2.36 to +0.12); counter -2.66, duo -0.41, outside zone -0.88, power -0.65. Brake episodes that never meet a blocker within 0.5 s: 29% (28.6 braked s across the snaps). History: the first trigger (any closing blocker) fired on 95% of snaps, and 54% of its brakes met nobody, mostly linemen locked on a DL that the backer was running toward; it cost outside zone +2.45 (Flex's check). Squaring to the climber was worse and is dropped. The quick scorecard's yd/carry 6.15 → 4.88 on the first trigger was game divergence. Seed 1909: impulse 320 → 220, he stays up, engaged (`test/backer-takeon.test.ts`). Not yet built: taking the block on with hands (a defender-started engagement via `sim.engage`; Flex). |
| `BACKER_CREEP` | on | promoted 2026-11-10 (run-ref-6; `=0` restores); was experimental | defense | `src/sim/backer-creep.ts`, `src/sim/playsim.ts` (stageDefense), `src/sim/behaviors/defense.ts` (unsure box backer) | set at first review. A box linebacker (2.5-7 yd off, within 6 yd of the ball, not a called blitzer) creeps into the snap with probability 0.2 + 0.4 × anticipation: from 0.3-0.6 s before the snap he walks downhill at 0.4-0.8 yd/s, and, having timed the count to do it, starts with the snap (no visual response). Still unsure after it, he keeps walking into his read at that pace (his read step is the creep continued) instead of planting, stepping back to a fixed read-step spot, or holding depth in the guard step. Not under a front stem. NFL box LBs in the last 0.1 s before the snap: 37-38% moving > 0.5 yd/s, about 60% of them downhill (median +0.4 yd/s), runs and dropbacks alike. Matched restage on `run-ref-3` @ 6c0bd5c0 (12 run-only games, 1,328 runs; `scripts/diag/_lbdepth.mts`, LBs 1.6-7 yd deep within 6 yd of the OL centre; NFL the same definition on the 2022 run-film archive), outside zone, all off / BACKER_CREEP / OFFBALL_TIMING / both / NFL: moving > 0.5 yd/s at the snap 5 / 23 / 5 / 23 / 46% (NFL about 30% downhill); speed at 0.2 s 0.00 / 0.00 / 0.34 / 0.47 / 0.81 yd/s; travel in the first second 1.31 / 1.38 / 1.36 / 1.41 / 1.94 yd; depth at 1.0 / 1.5 s 4.10/3.30, 3.93/3.08, 4.04/3.28, 3.91/3.06, NFL 3.56/2.82. Paired yards (capped at 20): BACKER_CREEP −0.02 (SE 0.07), OFFBALL_TIMING −0.03 (0.09), both −0.06 (0.10). Lateral at 1.0 s barely moves (0.95 → 0.98, NFL 1.58): the rest is acceleration from rest. Test `test/backer-start.test.ts`. |
| `OFFBALL_TIMING` | on | promoted 2026-11-10 (run-ref-6; `=0` restores); was experimental | defense | `src/sim/snap-count.ts` (planSnap, off-ball defenders) | set at first review. Under SNAP_REACT an off-ball defender may time the cadence (chance 0.3 + 0.5 × anticipation, never on a hard count): he starts with the snap, at a timing error of N(margin·sd, sd) clamped to [0, his visual response], never before the ball, instead of always at snapDelay + snapResponse (about 0.2 s). With the flag off no extra draw is made. See `BACKER_CREEP` for the evidence (same table). |
| `EDGE_REACH` | on | promoted 2026-11-10 (run-ref-8; `=0` restores); was experimental | defense | `src/sim/behaviors/defense.ts` (PassRusher key read), `src/sim/run-fits.ts` (edge fit) | set at first review. An edge (C/D gap) whose key reaches out toward him, away from the ball, answers it as an interior man answers his key: a flat step of his own at full effort the way the key goes, square, stepping from where he stands (outside his key), instead of the rush lane inside at the quarterback; a reach is a step mostly sideways (a drive step that drifts out, as on inside zone, is held), and his upfield pace on it is 1.2 yd/s, the NFL edge's own depth change. In his run fit, while the line flows to his side, a box edge's landmark never falls inside the outside shoulder of the man in front of him or inside his own alignment. Run Game's operators (`scripts/diag/edge-ops/`, NFL 2022 run-film archive vs matched restage, 373 OZ runs), stack (run-ref-4) / +EDGE_REACH / NFL: edge flow at 1.0 / 1.5 s −0.32/+0.41, +0.42/+0.92, +0.69/+1.75 yd; depth at 1.0 / 1.5 s −0.16/−0.23, +0.12/−0.20, −0.28/−0.81; blocker play side of him at 1.0 s 31 / 25 / 20%. Inside zone edge flow at 1.0 s −0.32 / +0.23 / +0.01 (open risk: he still widens a little where the NFL edge holds). Gun crossings B/C 45 / 50 / 34%, bounce 8 / 9 / 28% (the back's read is Run Game's). `npm run ref:report -- --flag EDGE_REACH=1` (fixed 1,321 runs): all +0.11 (SE 0.10), OZ +0.25 (0.24), gap +0.24 (0.17), IZ −0.41 (0.20). Test `test/edge-reach.test.ts`. |

| `READ_LEAN` | on | promoted 2026-11-10 (run-ref-10; `=0` restores); was experimental | defense | `src/sim/recognition.ts` (`lean`, `sawBall`), `src/sim/behaviors/defense.ts` (ZoneCoverage unsure box backer, `passPull` in runFit) | set at first review. A box backer acts on how his picture leans before he commits, not only after: while unsure, his downhill pace adds his lean (the probability his read would give from the filtered evidence he has seen, no situation prior, as −1 pass .. +1 run) times 1.5 yd/s toward the line on a run lean; a pass lean only takes his feet off the line (his pre-snap creep scaled by 1 + lean) and gives him no drop of his own: he drops once he reads pass, by his zone landmark and the receivers (a fixed 1.0 yd/s backpedal on a pass lean, in the first version, cost Coverage's closing in flight on dropbacks). After he commits to run and before he has seen the ball given or thrown, a pass lean (play action's line and back showing pass: set, no give) scales his downhill attack by 1 + lean, so the pass picture pulls him back as it builds. No timer; the evidence carries his own perception delay and filter. Defense's operator (`scripts/diag/align/readsplit.py`, box LB 1.6–7 yd deep, NFL 2022 run-film archive vs sim): run downhill at 0.5 / 0.6 s 0.61 / 0.82 yd/s (NFL 0.75 / 0.95); run trigger p50 0.7 s (NFL 0.6); run-vs-drop split 0.5 s (was 0.6; NFL 0.4; the rest is evidence onset); play-action downhill at 0.6 / 1.0 s 1.17 / 1.49 (was stronger than the run's; NFL 0.80 / 1.15). Dropback downhill at 0.4 / 0.8 / 1.0 s +0.05 / −0.49 / −0.52 (flag off +0.34 / −0.23 / −0.45; NFL +0.05 / −0.10 / −0.25): the over-drop by 1.0 s is mostly the zone drop after the pass read, there with the flag off. `npm run ref:report -- --flag READ_LEAN=1` on run-ref-8 (fixed 1,321 runs): all +0.02 (SE 0.07), OZ −0.13 (0.14), IZ −0.06 (0.18), gap +0.14 (0.14), duo +0.28 (0.21). Test `test/read-lean.test.ts`. |

| `ZONE_RUN` | on | promoted 2026-11-09 (run-ref-4; `=0` restores); was experimental | defense | `src/sim/engagement.ts:676` | set at first review. Keyed on the man in front's own first step (reach/bucket), an engaged interior DL opens his hips and races him for the play-side shoulder: open-hip speed (0.75 vmax) until outside it, then the man's pace plus a step. No line-flow gate. The race (and an upfield press at 1.5 yd/s while racing) runs only against a blocker seen running sideways at 1.5 yd/s or more, continuing while he keeps half that; a driving blocker gets the ordinary engaged read. The race ends for that blocker when the DL sees he is reached (the blocker's body half a body-width across his face to the play side) or the ball is past him downfield. Reached, he flattens: holds his contact depth and works down the line instead of pressing. Only a DL who lined up on or play side of his blocker can be reached; with ZONE_RUN on, BACKSIDE_SHOOT's cutoff press applied only to a DL shaded to his blocker's backside at the snap (`BACKSIDE_SHOOT` was removed 2026-11-10). Race press at the 0.5 lean (now RACE_LEAN): with ZONE_RUN, KEY_REACT, KEY_PACE, KEY_DEPTH = 1 on the matched restage, 1.5 -> 0.5 trades depth for drive-backs: DL depth at 2.0 s +0.76 -> +0.37 (NFL +0.70), driven back >= 0.5 from 0.5 s 10 -> 15% (NFL 17%), from snap 7 -> 9% (NFL 14%); no single press gives both, the spread is missing |

| `QB_RHYTHM` | on | promoted 2026-11-10 (run-ref-14; `=0` restores); was experimental (G3 QB, 2026-11-10; pair with `WR_SNAP`) | QB | `src/sim/behaviors/qb.ts` (progression, `outletThrow`) | set at first review. The progression keeps the QB's rhythm: a read that is not ready gets an ordinary look (no extra 0.6 s wait for a late route); once urgency passes the pressure mark (the rush he sees or his clock) his eyes go to the outlet (the last read); and moving off a read he takes a released outlet that is ready and clears his ordinary bar now. Measures behind it (BDB 2025 weeks 1-3 vs panel): NFL throws to backs 18.1% of targets at 2.50 s p50, ours 16-17% at 3.15 s; our backs release more (92% vs 74%) and sooner (leave the pocket 0.75 vs 1.40 s), so the back was late because he was read last; the QB clock (p50 3.77 s) sits at the NFL's give-up events (scramble 3.60, throwaway 3.90) and is left alone. 732 dropbacks with `WR_SNAP=1`, off -> on: sacks 42 -> 31 (both flags off 30), yards a pass play -0.01 +/- 0.39, INT 36 -> 35; backs 16.3 -> 23.7% of targets (NFL 18.1%: overshoot, logged) at 3.15 -> 2.90 s (NFL 2.50: partly), completed for <= 0 yd 3 -> 3, INT on throws to backs 4 -> 1. Second attempt; stopped per the two-attempt rule; when an open outlet should beat a later read needs its own diagnosis. Test `test/qb-rhythm.test.ts`. |
| `HONEST_READS` | on | promoted 2026-11-14 (engine-61; `=0` restores); was experimental (information-cheating audit, defense lane, 2026-11-13; intended on) | defense | `src/sim/behaviors/defense.ts` (Blitzer peel, `findsBall`, `Pursuit`, `takeOn`, DRIVE_CATCH in `playObservedFlight`), `src/sim/open-to-ball.ts`, `src/sim/breakdown.ts`, `src/sim/run-fits.ts` (FORCE_BOUNCE line), `src/sim/seen-contact.ts` (#693) | set at first review. Removes true-state reads the audit found, each replaced by what the player can perceive: a blitzer peels to the ball only once he has seen the throw, on his acquired flight (was the true flight on the true air state); the ball finder's head turn is measured from the thrower he sees (was the true thrower); a pursuer plays the ball only once he has seen it thrown (was any true air); a coverage player opens to the holder he has seen whatever the true ball state (was gated on true `held`); a breaking-down defender screens bodies by his picture (was true positions and true down state); a backer taking on a climber, and an edge setting his line, treat a body as blocked when their picture shows it in contact (`seenEngaged`, #693's seen contact; was its engagement state). The flight half (DRIVE_CATCH, `pursueIfLive`'s flight switches) is `HONEST_FLIGHT`. `executeRunFit` called without a read builds the read `runFit` builds (his acquired holder, else his mesh picture) instead of falling back to the true `sim.carrier` and `sim.handoffAt` (no production caller reaches it; the edge-backside-trail and edge-fit-approach tests do, and must pass explicit reads when the flag's old path is deleted). Not yet covered (structural, next): the acquired flight itself copies the true `ballPath`; the loose-ball chase and its assignment switch; tackle targeting of the true carrier; the windup tick. |
| `HONEST_FLIGHT` | off | experimental (information-cheating audit, the flight half; waits on the ball-perception channel built with Coverage) | defense | `src/sim/behaviors/defense.ts` (`pursueIfLive`, DRIVE_CATCH in `playObservedFlight`, `ballSeenInAir`), `src/sim/possession-read.ts` (`flightLanded`) | set at first review. A defender drives on a catch at the receiver his own flight estimate brings the ball to (from 0.05 s before its arrival to 0.4 s after, unless he sees the receiver without the ball), at the facing he sees (was the private securing state and the true facing); `pursueIfLive` switches from playing the flight to the catch (catch-carry, flight continuation, ball rally) when his own estimate says the ball has arrived and he does not see it in the air near him (`flightLanded`, `ballSeenInAir`: within 6 yd and in view; was the true end of the air state); a flight he has acquired is his to play until his estimate resolves it. Held back from `HONEST_READS`: with it on, how a defender learns a catch is secured or lost needs the ball estimate (acquireFlight still copies the true path), and catch-carry's tests (caught / dropped) are not yet restated for it. |
| `HONEST_TACKLE` | on | promoted 2026-11-14 (engine-61; `=0` restores); was experimental (information-cheating audit, defense lane, tackle targeting; 2026-11-13) | defense | `src/sim/tackle.ts` (`tryTackles`) | set at first review. The tackler's decisions come from his picture of the carrier, not the true state: he reaches only for a body his picture does not show without the ball (a back he has not yet seen take the handoff is not his target until he sees it; unknown possession still counts), and his closing read for the last reach and his separating and behind reads for the dive use the carrier's seen position and velocity (were the true `sim.carrier` for every defender, and its true motion). Reach, grip, contact quality and the dive itself stay physical. Not covered: a defender cannot tackle a body without the ball (a faked back he believes has it); that needs a non-carrier contact outcome. The tackle-window preview's picture is Rules+Coaching's 1a'. Fresh run set (engine-59, 4 placebos): all +0.04 (SE 0.03) vs placebo +0.05 / RMS 0.06, 9% of plays changed, missed + broken 0.305 -> 0.280 a run. Where it holds a reach (4 league games, a tackler within 2.5 yd of the carrier his picture shows without the ball): 131 of 312 dropbacks (the catch, before he sees it), 34 of 97 play-action, 19 of 140 runs. Why missed + broken falls (split, fresh set): the possession hold alone 0.307 (1% of plays change), the seen-motion reads alone 0.277; the escape reads (dive: separating and behind; last reach: closing) now come from his delayed picture, which does not yet show a runner's burst away, so he fires fewer low-percentage layouts and lunges before he could have seen the burst (4 games, decision ticks where truth and picture disagree: dive 45 truth-only vs 2 picture-only, picture separation p50 0.94 vs true 1.97 yd/s; last reach 22 vs 5); yards unchanged. Play action and RPO (forced panel, gun doubles, Cover 1-4, seeds 1-6, both flips, paired): a faked back draws no reach in either arm (no non-carrier contact outcome), no fake holds a rusher off the QB (first rusher contact before the throw identical on 33 plays; sacks 7 -> 7), the 41 PA holds are at the catch; the RPO gave the run on all 48 (pre-snap read), identical to IZ. Not yet honest about identity: the picture's possession bit is the true holder, delayed (`perception.record(..., sim.carrier.idx)`), so no fake can be believed; the presented-possession channel (approved 2026-11-14, after HONEST_READS) replaces it. Test `test/honest-tackle.test.ts`. |

Each read's step index comes from the plan (`plan.rhythm[].n`: 0 the set, 1 the first hitch, 2 the second; reads on one look share an `n`); he lands step n n strides of his own drop after his set (a stride: the drop's time over its taught steps). His eyes leave for the next read one stride before its step lands, never before his yes/no glance on the read he is on. The hitch is a base reset at depth, turned to the read (a forward hitch is bit 4's pressure decision). A read is thrown once its step has landed or with the landing inside his load; under pressure (urgency 0.8+) from where he is. A vertical is readable when its release is decided in his pictures (out of the collision, even with or past the man over him), not at 12 yd. Off the plan's steps, the dwell as before; triangle, peek, key and hot reads unchanged. Paired staged read against #780 (engine-69): sacks smash 12 -> 14, mesh 1 -> 2, dagger 22 -> 21, drive 6 -> 4, flood 7 -> 0; yd a play drive +0.8, double_outs +2.6, mesh -2.7 (its corner, read 1 on the set, not yet open: the grass read, unbuilt). Bit 2: the drop's body (a crossover side-on toward read 1's side; on the plant stride he turns to his picture of read 1; the drop paced to its taught time; his requested facing turns on the plant, a landed step or the throw, not on a look alone; the play-action fake held to the measured mesh, now bit 16). Bit 16 (split from bit 2, 2026-11-16): the play-action fake held to the measured mesh (gun 1.45 s, under center 1.5 s; off 0.7 s); lands only with PA reads decided on the plant out of the fake (alone, staged PA sacks 14.8 -> 27.1%: he plants at 2.3 s, the NFL's 2.2, then reads like a dropback). Bit 8 (with 1 and 2): the grass read. A first read on a route into space (`GRASS_ROUTES`, the set agreed with Rules from the sources: go and fade; the corner as read 1 and not half of a key; not the seam at the plant, nor the post or deep out, thrown on the break) is watched on its landing area (the forecast 1.2 s ahead) through the drop and decided by the plant: readable once out of the collision with nobody in his picture between the man and the landing area (a man shallower than the receiver is under the throw), with an awareness-scaled judgment margin; priced as any read; not thrown on the plant, he is on to read 2 with no dwell and no look-off. Staged against bits 1+2 (192 plays a concept): mesh corners 5 -> 20 (2.42 s, 9.4 yd; were 3.04 s, 12.6 yd), mesh sacks 6 -> 0, smash sacks 17 -> 11 and interceptions 8 -> 4; dagger and the seam concepts unchanged (the seam is a hook/safety read off the hitch, not a grass read). With Rules' `GRASS_READ=1` (#802) the plan is the source: which reads are grass reads (any read in the rhythm: declared verticals, and the corner when it is read 1 and not half a key), the landing area (the NFL median air yards on the installed route) and the step `n` that decides it; without it, the fallback above, its landing his forecast at a ball's arrival (his wind-up plus its flight). Staged with the plan's fields: four verts verticals 3.0 s / 12.6 yd -> 2.69 s / 10.1 yd (NFL 2.6 s, 9.2 yd), sacks four verts 20 -> 11 and dagger 22 -> 11, smash as off (its corner is keyed). | `BLITZ_READ` | on | promoted 2026-11-16 (engine-72; `=0` restores); was experimental (post-snap blitz read, 2026-11-13; reshaped to a rush belief, work in progress) | QB | `src/sim/behaviors/qb.ts` (`freeRusherSeen`, the hot block before the read) | set at first review. A rusher his protection does not account for, coming at him unblocked in his picture in the first second and held for his processing time, sends his eyes and the ball to the hot. Speed cue (two attempts): it fired on almost none of the free-rusher blitzes, his picture's closing speed lagging the truth by about 0.4 s. Trigger cue (a second-level defender's step toward the line from his snap spot, watched from the snap; a recognized free rusher ends the drop): on free-rusher blitzes quick throws 9 -> 24-36% and sacks 9 -> 0-4%, but it also fires on 62-74% of non-blitz plays, on coverage players coming up on short routes (man corners, zone backers and safeties); the cue still needs his direction at the QB through the box or off the edge. Attempt 3 (a pre-snap count of box and edge threats, the trigger a step at him, a late rotation by recognition): free-rusher blitzes quick 5 -> 16%, sacks 12.7 -> 7.6%, but false hots on 25% of non-blitz plays, on box linebackers stepping up on run looks or in man on the back; stopped. docs/reviews/2026-11-13-qb-gather.md. Reshaped (the user's direction): a per-defender rush belief from alignment, updated from position change in his felt picture at his awareness's rate; believed excess over the protection raises urgency, discounts believed rushers in his window, and puts his eyes first on the vacated side. 16 games: quick throws blitz 5 -> 13% (NFL 18), plain 4 -> 10% (9); free-rusher sacks 8.5 -> 5.5%. The prior's numbers are estimates: realized rush by snap class (sim / NFL without OLB) line off-ball 81 / 42% (LB 54, DB 27), box 10 / 11%, edge - / 19%, else 1 / 1%, so box 0.25 is about double and the line prior should split by role (`nfl_rush_by_align.py`, `blitz-trap.ts`). Trap: look-prior throws into a refilled seat 53% comp, 4.6 yd/att against 72%, 8.4 open; no interception yet by the re-dealt defender. Urgency is raised on 33% of plain plays (56% play action: box backers stepping up on the fake), costing 0.33 s of time to throw and no shorter looks. |

The forecast already moves his own body through the World and holds the runner's seen velocity; on a runner as fast as he is (or accelerating past his seen, stale velocity) its best point always sat at the last sample, so each tick it promised a better shot 0.3 s away and he never took the one he had (man-trail-carry cover0 mesh s5: the SS keeps his feet from 2.90 to 3.40 s at quality 0.0-0.34 against a forecast 0.41-0.52 at 0.30 s, closing 0.04 yd a tick; with the wait removed he wraps at 2.95 s). A single added rusher behind three-deep is a five-man zone pressure with three under; where the front already rushes an extra man (an on-line Sam, a 3-4's fifth lineman, Penny's walked-up Will) Mike-A / Will-B / Nickel Edge in Cover 3 became six rush and two curl-flat players with no one in the middle, by accident. Under the flag those calls carry `maxRushers: { cover3: 5 }` and the installer refuses a composition over it; it also counts the backer alignDefense walks up onto the line (Penny) as a rusher, as the installer does, so promised counts elsewhere (Deuce, Slug, Sting, the mug calls in Penny fronts) are checked against the rush they really install. Static check (every installable pressure x coverage x front x 4 formations): accidental six-rush Cover 3 installs 60 -> 0, no other installable call changed except those Penny-front calls that broke their promise. Not a ban on six-rush Cover 3: a designed three-deep two-under pressure is parked (docs/PARKED.md, `MAX_ZONE_6`). Test `test/rush-promise.test.ts`. |
| `QB_RELEASE` | off | experimental (QB out of the pocket as a live passer, P3 part (i), 2026-11-13) | defense | `src/sim/possession-read.ts` (`qbRunningAtLine`), `src/sim/behaviors/defense.ts` (`pursueIfLive`) | set at first review. An underneath zone defender comes up on a QB seen running only when his own picture shows the QB committed to the line: within 3 yd of it moving at it at 2.5+ yd/s, or past it; once released he stays released while the QB is still seen running. Off, `qbRunning`'s outside-the-box clause (1.5 yd outside the pictured tackles, downfield 1+ yd/s) released every underneath zone on a QB drifting wide as a passer. Rushers and contain players keep `qbRunning`; man and deep defenders already plaster to the line. Scripted-QB scenario (`scripts/diag/defense/qb-release.ts`, curl_flat from 2x2 vs Cover 2/3/4, seeds 1-6): rollout drifting 4-7 yd deep, underneath zones released 49/72 off, 0/72 on; true scramble up the middle identical (same releases where he got out, same yards); drift then run, release 2.95 -> 3.7 s, yards mean 4.5 -> 6.7 (p50 6.3 -> 9.8, max 13.8 -> 19.1), the cost of waiting for the line. The tuck (ball tucked vs held to throw) is not yet in the picture; when the QB lane adds it, it joins the release. Tests `test/qb-running-cue.test.ts`. |
| `READ_PLAN` | on | promoted 2026-11-12 (engine-35; `=0` restores); was experimental (read-compiler Phase 1, 2026-11-11; needs `FORECAST_BREAK`, on since engine-32) | QB | `src/sim/behaviors/qb.ts` (`initPlan`, `readKey`, the triangle and alert reads); `src/playbook/read-compiler/runtime.ts` | set at first review. The QB runs the read plan compiled from the routes our engine runs (`src/playbook/read-compiler`; compiled once per concept and formation with generic teams and parked defenders, cached) instead of the hand-written `progression`. Plans (structural rules; HonestNFL sheets as the reference): a defender key where a high-low's low route is ready on rhythm before the high one declares, or a clear-and-void pair (a deep key must be deeper than the low route); a vertical peek (two or more verticals on the start side and a released back: one look that lasts until every vertical is 12 yd deep plus his processing, staying on one close to his bar, then the back); a triangle read as one picture; a yes/no alert on an isolated vertical away from the start side; otherwise pure progression, the start side through before the other side, the breaking route before the settling one, the back a read at his window on the side he releases to (he may be first). The key is read from the drop in his own delayed picture with his decision-making's noise. Under a plan QB_RHYTHM's rhythm outlet is off; pressure still sends the eyes to the back. Paired forced-concept panel (engine-32, `BACK_RELEASE` on, 1,536 plays): sacks 74 -> 68, INT 45 -> 43, yards a play 9.67 -> 9.96; the sheets' answer dagger/C2 dig .33 -> .58, sail-corner/C3 .50 -> .91, curl-flat/C3 .75 -> 1.00; four verts: back .26 -> .37 (NFL .32 on 3+ verticals), vertical .26 -> .33. Known and accepted: the back open and never read (.25 of drop-backs); no second key or triangle for flood, comebacks, pa_post_dig (honest picks by unread defenders); the corner vs Cover 2 rarely thrown (half-safety coverage and the slot corner's late, straight stem, outside the flag); hot answers wait on the QB seeing a free rusher (QB_FEEL's perception half). Needs `FORECAST_BREAK` (on since engine-32); read with `BACK_RELEASE` (on since engine-34). Evidence `docs/reviews/2026-11-11-read-compiler-phase1.md`. Tests `test/read-plan.test.ts`, `test/read-compiler.test.ts`. |
| `HOT_LOOK` | on | promoted 2026-11-12 (engine-41; `=0` restores); was experimental (hot-throw follow-up, 2026-11-12; replaces the retired `HOT_PLAN`, which as built never fired: docs/reviews/2026-11-12-hot-look.md) | QB | `src/sim/behaviors/qb.ts` (`hotLook`, `inClearLook`, `gazePoint`, the QB_RHYTHM pressure jump) | set at first review. The hot throw costs the look: under an alarm he picks a target from his pictures (as old as his last look at each); one inside his clear look is thrown after the windup, anyone else first needs his eyes there (head turn at an assumed 400°/s plus a glance set by decision-making and awareness) while he evades, and is judged again on the fresh picture. Nothing barred; no throw in time leaves him to evade, scramble or throw away as before. Also closes a peek or triangle and stops watching an unread key when pressure sends his eyes to the back (off: his gaze stayed on the verticals while his read was the back). Off: the instant best of every receiver on the alarm tick. |
| `DEF_BELIEF` | on | promoted 2026-11-12 (engine-43; `=0` restores); was experimental (risk work, the window's defender model, 2026-11-12; calibrated on engine-40's defense, re-read when it changes) | QB | `src/sim/behaviors/qb.ts` (`window`) | set at first review. His belief about how defenders close on a throw: a defender within 4 yd of the target moves partly with him over his blind time (PHASE_MEAN 0.5; off, every defender keeps his course), and a defender arriving just after the ball, inside the securing window (SECURE_T 0.3 s), can still get it out (DISLODGE, by the receiver's hands and contested-catch skill). Right on average; spread both ways by inexperience (decision-making and experience). PHASE_MEAN, SECURE_T and DISLODGE are his beliefs about our defense and catch model, calibrated on engine-41 with ARRIVE_LEAD, CUSHION_BREAK, CATCH_SECURE, CATCH_HANDS, LOOK_ONCE and DRIVE_CATCH on (and SHOULDER_READ off; checked with LINE_START on): a change to any of them (or match-carry, SHOULDER_READ on) needs a re-read of `calibration_report.py` |
| `ACC_BELIEF` | on | promoted 2026-11-12 (engine-46; `=0` restores); was experimental (risk work, his belief of his own accuracy, 2026-11-12) | QB | `src/sim/behaviors/qb.ts` (`window`) | set at first review. The window's miss prices the throw he will release: the urgency of the rush as he will feel it when the ball leaves (his picture of the nearest rusher, closed over the windup: `pressure.time - wind`), unscaled by pocket presence (that stays on his bar), and a flick when that rusher is on him by the release (`pressure.time - wind < 0.15 s`). How well he anticipates it is his knowledge (decision-making and experience): an error both ways of up to about 0.25 in urgency for the least experienced. Off: the decision's urgency, scaled by pocket presence, the rush as it is now, no flick (docs/reviews/2026-11-12-accuracy-belief.md) |

Flat: run 4.5 yd behind the line while widening, climbing late (NFL depth -4.6 / -3.7 yd at 1.5 / 2.5 s, width 5.4 / 10.3 yd; ours on: -4.5 / -3.7, 5.6 / 11.3; off: -2.9 / +0.8, 4.0 / 7.1); caught at a median -0.1 yd, 50% behind the line (NFL -1.0, 56%; off +2.3, 6%). Angle and out break at the line: caught at +2.7 / +2.4 yd (NFL +3 / +2; off +4.4 / +5.3). Yards per attempt to the back barely move (the YAC gap is elsewhere) (docs/reviews/2026-11-13-back-paths.md). | `SCREEN_CALL` | on | promoted 2026-11-14 (engine-66; `=0` restores); was experimental (menu sizing, 2026-11-14) | play calling | `src/game/playcaller.ts` (`SCREEN_CALL_RATE`, `quickScreensFor`) | set at first review. Pool admission only: the quick screens (bubble from trips, bunch now from bunch, now screen from 2x2, tunnel from 2x2 or trips) and the screen RPO (inside zone / now screen, gun doubles 11, its pre-snap read `QBMind.rpoRead` unchanged) become calls of their own at the NFL's rate by down and distance, each drawn on its own stream so the rest of the call is unchanged; screens are called without motion (their blocking is built from the formation as aligned). Rates (BDB 2025 weeks 1-9): the targeted, non-RPO WR-screen rate per dropback (2.6 / 1.5 / 3.0 / 3.8% on 1st, 2nd short/medium/long; 1.0 / 0.9 / 4.3% on 3rd and 4th) divided by the share of our pass calls whose formation fits (67-83%, engine-62 collect), and the RPO's share of run-or-RPO calls (21-37%), from 11 personnel only. Why: on engine-62 our caller calls no screen and no RPO (SCREEN 0 vs 4.2% of NFL routes run, 9.8% of targets); the screens were kept in the screen lab. Ours is the screen-only RPO; 65% of NFL RPO throws are not screens (flat 18%, slant 11%, out 10%). 16 games (seed 101, engine-62 default + flag), 1,629 pass plays: quick screens 3.0% of pass plays (tunnel 1.5, bunch now 0.6, bubble 0.4, now 0.4; 2 killed to slant-flat), completed 71-100%, mean yards tunnel 4.6, now 1.7, bunch now 8.0, bubble 14.7 (small n); RPO called on 12.9% of run-or-RPO calls (NFL 24%: 11 personnel only), thrown 73% (NFL 30% of all RPOs), 95% completed, 9.0 yd mean, kept as a run 3.7. Promoted when a screen in a game looks like one (its blocking, the catch and the defense's rally), not when the share matches; the defense's response by coverage on its existing rules (holdFlat, cloud corner, apex) is the next read. Test `test/screen-call.test.ts`. |
| `STALK_CLOSE` | on | promoted 2026-11-14 (engine-66; `=0` restores); was experimental (screens, 2026-11-14; attempt 1 of 2) | screens | `src/sim/behaviors/screen.ts` (`ScreenBlocker.update`) | set at first review. A receiver's stalk as coached: before the ball is out he closes the cushion under control to a breakdown point 1.5 yd off his man on the man's line to the catch (a coaching figure), from what he sees of the man, then mirrors and puts his hands on the man's catch-side half; off, he worked to leverage no more than 0.5 yd past his alignment at 0.45 speed and let the man come. Why: on the now screen the stalk engaged no one on 71-75% of plays (screen-defense.mts, nickel Over, seeds 1-12 x both flips, SCREEN_QB=1); he set, came within 1.1 yd and the nickel ran by. Perimeter stalk on a DB (stalk_reference.py, BDB 2025 wk 1-6 vs screen lab): gap at 0.9 / 1.2 s NFL 2.0 / 1.1 yd, off 5.1 / 4.7, on 4.4 / 3.7; first contact before the catch NFL 1.0 s, off 0.0, on 0.5; never NFL 1 of 18, off 50 of 263, on 63 of 313. The rest of the gap starts at the snap: the stalk and his man begin 5.2 yd apart (NFL 3.7; our now-screen receiver is split 15.7 yd from the ball, NFL 13.1). It is not the man holding depth: over WR screens the NFL's man over the receiver goes from 4.3 to 4.6 yd deep by the throw on the now screen and 1.8 to 1.4 on the bubble (quick_screen_reference.py), as ours does; stalk_reference.py's "man down 1.1 yd by 1.2 s" is the man the stalk ends up engaging (selected by contact), not the flat defender. A flat-defender squeeze (FLAT_SQUEEZE, tried 2026-11-14, not kept) brought ours down 0.6 yd by the throw, more than the NFL. Stalk engagements (4 quick screens x 5 coverages x 24): now 50 -> 112, tunnel 54 -> 116; from behind 0-3% both arms. Now screen, stalk engaged no one 71-75% -> 8-25%; yards p50 Cover 3 2.4 -> 3.1, Quarters 0.1 -> 8.0 (NFL 5-6), tackler spread in Quarters CB 46 / FS 29 / NB 21% (NFL CB 47, ILB 25, FS 19, SS 14); open: Cover 3's nickel still wins through 63% (the block itself), Cover 2's squat corner makes every tackle (the coached answer is the kill against cloud; our kill reads press only), Cloud's breakaways (p50 19.7: the free-runner rally, Defense's). **Attempt 2 (2026-11-14), not kept:** the engaged stalk mirroring his man instead of `stand()` changed nothing (an engaged blocker's request does not move him; the contact does). Cover 3's losses are contact physics: the stalk engages 0.65 s before the catch but the contact ends after 0.45 s by separation (14 of 15; isolated run contact, grace 0, an arm apart), the nickel arriving at 3.3 yd/s against the stalk's 1.0; NFL contact holds to the catch. For the contact owner (does a receiver's grip cost a moving DB his momentum) and Defense (a DB playing the blocker breaks down and sheds rather than running through). Test `test/stalk-close.test.ts`. |

A dead quick screen (catcher covered or lane filled, in his picture, for his patience) is thrown away on his read; a clock one second past the timing is only the outer bound. A to-spot throw is rechecked by the rule it was thrown on and released to the spot (tunnel thrown 2.7 -> 1.8 s, NFL 1.6), and naturalFlight is refit to BDB 2025 pass_arrived. The tunnel's sell (behind the line), route and split come from tracking (`routes.ts`, `alignment.ts`, `assign.ts`): WR-in caught 40 -> 60% (NFL 91) (docs/reviews/2026-11-13-screen-qb.md) |

| `INT_BELIEF` | on | promoted 2026-11-12 (engine-48; `=0` restores); was experimental (risk work, his belief of interception risk, 2026-11-12) | QB | `src/sim/behaviors/qb.ts` (`window`) | set at first review. His interception risk is built as picks happen: a touch on the ball in flight ends a pick a fraction of the time (path terms ×`PATH_PICK` 0.2), the catch point's jumped-route and contest terms are weighted up (×`CATCH_PICK` 1.75), and defenders combine as probabilities, not a sum. How well he prices it is his knowledge (decision-making and experience): a two-sided multiplicative error of up to about ±30% in log for the least experienced. Weights fitted by likelihood on natural league throws (seeds 101/201, checked on 301/401) on the engine-47 candidate: calibrated against CATCH_CARRY, HANDS_CUE, CATCH_SPEED and CATCH_CHOICE on, and engine-46's defense with DEF_BELIEF on; re-read when the catch, tip or coverage rules change. 32 games per arm: priced over 5% 6.8% / 4.1% observed off, 6.4% / 5.1% on; sacks 131 → 121, yards a play 8.05 → 8.23, INT 46 → 49. Off: the summed terms (docs/reviews/2026-11-12-int-belief.md) |

Sources: Smart Football (the shallow cross needs a full five-step; the slant is three-step), Throw Deep (stick from one to three steps), Coiner (comebacks five steps and a hitch), Gonzalez via Smart Football (four verticals a quick five, read 1 on the last step: ours is not ready until 2.7, a route timing question for the QB lane); the rest by the read-plan table's analogues. Only marks the class: 'five' and 'gun5' are the same drop in the gun until the QB lane sets 'five' to the NFL's depth and time in `dropSpec`. **Revised (2026-11-14, after the QB lane's dropSpec('five') read: 3.5-5 yd drops 13% -> 45% against the NFL's 28%):** four_verts and verts_hitch go to the gun three-step, as coached ("a quick five-step drop (from shotgun, a quick three-steps)", Gonzalez via Smart Football); the other ten keep 'five' pending the call-mix refit. Our routes are not ready late (route-time.py, BDB 2025 wk 1-6 vs engine default, same operator: depth past the line at 2.0 / 2.5 s GO 7.2 / 11.1 vs NFL 6.2 / 9.6, POST 7.3 / 11.1 vs 7.3 / 10.7, CORNER 7.3 / 11.0 vs 7.1 / 10.4, IN 6.7 / 9.7 vs 6.1 / 9.2, OUT 5.5 / 6.9 vs 5.2 / 7.7; breaks OUT 2.3 vs 2.1 s, POST 2.9 vs 2.7), so the five-step class gets its own beat in the plan's rhythm: read 1 on the hitch, 0.8 s after the set (the NFL's set-to-release at 3.5-5 yd: set 2.2 s, ball out 3.0), then a 0.4 s beat; the quick game keeps 0.4. Under center our only middle drop is 6.4 yd behind the snap (NFL five-step mostly 6.5-8.5); the quick drop exists (3.4 yd) and quick concepts are called from under center (22 of 203 under-center pass calls, 16 games) but rarely: a call-mix item, not a missing drop. Test `test/concept-drops.test.ts`. **Re-marked (2026-11-15; docs/reviews/2026-11-15-concept-drops.md):** by the first read's route, as Walsh times it (the square-out on five quick steps, the curl five-step timed: read 1 on the set, 'gun5'; the square-in and the post five steps and a hitch, the deep comeback a seven with a hitch: 'five'), and Pina's gun equivalents. 'five': smash, mesh, drive, corner_flat, comebacks, and levels (added: its square-in is ready 2.9-3.0 s); back to 'gun5': flood, sail_corner, double_outs, trips_sail, trips_outs (an out or the flat first, ready 1.9-2.3 s). Reads within 0.45 s of ready (installedPlan rhythm, all pass concepts but screens): off 58 / early 28 / late 10; as marked before 50 / 18 / 28; re-marked 58 / 18 / 20. Not a drop: the verticals' ready time in the quick game, the PA beat, and read 2 of a keyed high-low on 'five' (QB lane). |

| `CRAFT_HOLD` | on | promoted 2026-11-16 (engine-73; `=0` restores); was turned back off 2026-11-16 (engine-71) after one engine on: it bends receivers off the quarterback\'s aim and overrides the stick\'s break (Coverage, 289 paired throws: 20 catches lost, 6 won); second attempt with Rules; was experimental (routes, 2026-11-15; Flex's dither, Integration ruling 1) | routes | `src/sim/behaviors/receiver.ts` (`routeCraft`, `RouteRunner.routeSide`), `src/sim/route-motion.ts` (`steerRoute`) | set at first review. The route craft commits to the side it chose round a man in his lane and keeps it until he has been clear of him for 0.2 s: the side is his route's from the snap when it is clear (the declared break's leg, with DECLARED_BREAK), else the side he already holds (kept as a field direction), else the per-tick break side. The bend never takes him back down his route (at least 0.3 of his pace along it) or across to the other side of the man. Off, the side came from a break side re-derived every tick from his velocity, so a wing pressed against an end flipped his request +-7 yd/s across nearly every tick (gun_wing_12 cover6, 1.0-1.7 s). A receiver bent off his stem rejoins his route ahead: a point he is beyond along both its legs is passed however far to the side, and a straight-through marker (a turn under 12 deg) is skipped once he is a yard off its leg (the cover6 seam released 2.9 yd outside the end and cut back flat to his 4-yd point, then ran back down his route to it). The held side is a commitment about one man and ends with him. It ends, with no run-out, when he is past that man in his picture: level or behind, or stepped across his line onto the held side and out of his lane, unless that side is the route's declared one (RELEASE_SIDE's wing slant goes under the end whichever way the end moves). A man beside him on the far side is the commitment working, and the hold stays. At his route's declared break the hold is over and the craft is the per-tick one, so the break is the route's. A declared leg that comes back toward the line (a stick, hook, curl or comeback settling: at least 0.3 of it backward) names no side to hold; an out's leg (0.04 backward) is a break toward the sideline and names its side. Coverage's paired staged throws (`scripts/diag/catch/paired-throws.ts`, 8 concepts x 5 coverages x 8 seeds, gun doubles, the oracle receiver), engine-71 tree (develop e90c204c): CRAFT_HOLD alone against off, 289 pairs, 197 vs 209 caught, 19 lost (seam 5, corner 5, stick 4, shallow_high 3, hook 2) and 7 won. With CRAFT_EXIT, 302 pairs, 217 vs 217, 8 lost (seam 2, shallow_high 2, corner 2, hook 1, back_flat 1) and 8 won; against the hold alone, 15 won and 4 lost. Of the 8, the two seams, the hook and the back_flat run the same path through the release (the defence and the aim differ, from other receivers' crafts); the two corners and two crossers are the hold choosing as built (the corner round the nickel on his break's side, 13-17 degrees, where off bends 50 degrees inside; the crosser keeping his side where off flips it). Dither budget on the defaults (reversed re-aims/s, hold off / hold alone / with CRAFT_EXIT): route controller 0.140 / 0.077 / 0.096, release 0.088 / 0.038 / 0.034, carrier controller 0.357 / 0.377 / 0.370, carrier spacing 0.097 / 0.102 / 0.100; within budget in every arm. | `PERCEIVE_TRACK` | off | experimental (perception channels, step 0, 2026-11-13) | engine | `src/sim/playsim.ts` (`track`) | set at first review. The tracking channel: `track(observer, target)` sees an attended target through a short correcting loop (TRACK_LATENCY 0.12 s, chosen inside the 0.1-0.15 s evidence) with no focus, stress or attention terms stacked on it and no acceleration term, instead of `see()`'s choice reaction time. Step 0 is the entry point only (off: exactly `see()`; no consumer calls it yet); each consumer group moves to it in its own step with its own restage and retires its own compensator (step 1a pursuit and the tackle window first). **Sight (2026-11-14, Integration's ruling (a)):** the loop corrects only on a target in sight: in contact, or in view (the gaze cone under GAZE, else 1.8 rad either side of body facing, as the QB's feel) with no body's centre line across the sight line (`inSight`). Its picture is the newest clear moment that has had the latency to arrive, carried forward on its velocity: behind a body, and for one latency after the line reopens, it ages from the last clear sample; one older than `see()`'s picture is replaced by `see()`. Before this, step 0 gave a clean picture of a man behind a body or behind the observer's shoulder (chase read: 33% / 4.3% of calls, `scripts/diag/eyes/track-geometry.ts`): a true-state leak in the entry point, found by TRACK_TACKLE attempt 2. No group is on in develop or the engine-59 candidate, so nothing landed was affected; each group's read is redone on it. TRACK_LATENCY 0.12 s is sampled in whole ticks (0.10 s at dt 0.05). docs/reviews/2026-11-13-perception-proposal.md |

Boundary agreed with Defense: who he tackles (`observedHolder`) and the commit reads (`closing`, `separating`, `behind`) are Defense's, under its own flag, read from the same source. Witnesses: close-range re-steer at 1.5-3 yd (`pursuit_aim.py`; about 20% against the NFL's 6%), the last 1.5 yd against the NFL (`scripts/diag/pursuit/last_yards.py`), closest approach and approach speeds, broken tackles by approach angle, the tackle panel (`scripts/diag/tackle/panel.ts`, reach-commit time and dive rate), missed + broken a run with placebo, dither-budget. Attempt 1 (2026-11-13, engine-58 + step 0, off -> on): a miss, parked. #700's close-resteer split (8 games, seed 101): close re-steer 8.1 -> 8.3% of 1.5-3 yd chase ticks; close-gather 224 -> 210, handover 123 -> 135, spacing 74 -> 76% of re-steers (spacing is SPACING_SMOOTH's; with TRACK_PURSUIT too: 7.8%). Fresh run set: all -0.12 yd a run (SE 0.05) against the placebo mean +0.04 (RMS 0.05), about 3 RMS below; missed + broken 0.306 -> 0.303. dither-budget fails: carrier / spacing reversed re-aims 0.122 -> 0.180/s (budget 0.174). `pursuit_aim.py` close re-steer 19.7 / 20.6 -> 21.0 / 22.3%. Next (attempt 2): on top of Defense's tackle-window forecast fix (waitForBetter forecasting his attainable motion, not his request), after 1b. **Attempt 2 (2026-11-14), on Defense's WAIT_REACHED (#708, quality rule, on in its stack) with the sighted track() (scratch/wait-reached-track2):** step 0's track() had given a clean picture through bodies and behind the shoulder (33% / 4.3% of chase reads); fixed in the entry point (#695). Tests (tackle-window, dither-budget, man-trail-carry, pursuit-commit, perceive-track): 41 / 41 off and on. Fresh run set: all -0.07 yd a run (SE 0.05) against the placebo mean +0.05 (RMS 0.06), every family inside its floor (OZ -0.19 / -0.19 / 0.25, draw -0.17 / +0.52 / 0.55); missed 0.228 -> 0.227 (attempt 1 before the sight fix: 0.242), broken 0.116 -> 0.105, missed + broken 0.305 -> 0.293. By read (site filter): chase close-gather -0.07 (0.05), the tryTackles window -0.03 (0.03), whiffMargin inert in this stack. The sight fix removed the miss rise; what remains is fewer broken tackles from a fresher picture of the carrier at the close. |
| `SIGHT_CARRIER` | on | promoted 2026-11-14 (engine-63; `=0` restores); was experimental (SEE_SIGHT step 1, information rule, 2026-11-14; intended on once measured) | ball carrier | `src/sim/playsim.ts` (`see`, `sighted`), `src/sim/sight.ts` (`lineOfSight`) | set at first review. The ball carrier's picture of an opponent he cannot see (behind his shoulders, beyond 1.8 rad of body facing with GAZE off, or behind a body by `lineOfSight`'s heights and postures) is the newest moment, at least his ordinary delay back, at which he had the man in sight (in view by his recorded facing then, no body across the line by `lineOfSight`'s heights with everyone where the perception record has him then), carried forward on its velocity: a man he cannot see ages from when he last saw him and is picked up one delay after he is back in sight; beyond the record (1.2 s), the last picture he had. Visibility at a past tick never changes, so it is kept per tick. Before it, about half his traffic reads (`trafficTempo`, the carrier's lane and cut reads) were fresh delayed pictures of men behind him (`scripts/diag/eyes/see-sight.ts`: 50% / 46% behind, 20% through a body). With GAZE on, the view half is GAZE's own memory and this adds the occlusion; turning GAZE on for the carrier would let him glance back at the cost of his head off the lane. Witnesses: YAC and yards after contact, runs' 10+ and 20+ share, the carrier's cut and tempo reaction to a pursuer behind (staged); controls: the fresh run set with placebo, dither-budget. **Attempt 1, first build (2026-11-14):** kept memory only from the carrier's own sighted calls, so at the handoff a man he could not see had a cold 1.2 s-old picture (7.4% of his pictures): a defect, replaced by the record-based rule before any read was taken. Its fresh set (all -0.26, SE 0.09) is not the mechanism's read. **Read 1 on the record-based rule (2026-11-14, develop engine-60, off -> on):** fresh run set all -0.04 yd a run (SE 0.08) against the placebo mean +0.05 (RMS 0.06), every family inside its floor (duo -0.25 against +0.12 / 0.24; OZ -0.30 against -0.18 / 0.25); missed + broken 0.305 -> 0.314. Short-catch (0-10 yd) YAC, seeds 101 / 201 x 8: mean 6.19 / 6.79 -> 5.98 / 5.30 (pooled about 6.47 -> 5.66, NFL 3.91), p75 6.69 / 6.29 -> 5.78 / 5.67; yards a run 3.90 / 3.98 -> 3.89 / 3.89. `dither-budget` and the sight tests pass off and on (9 / 9). A catcher who cannot see the man closing from behind is run down more often; runs barely move. |

Seed 11's +0.9 underneath was game divergence (Coverage's localisation). Neutral on passing: a crosser is behind a body for a few ticks and his picture ages about 0.15 s, which the zone defender's drive absorbs. **Run row (Integration's ask, 2026-11-14):** exposure is real (`scripts/diag/eyes/cover-run-exposure.ts`, 101 x 2: 61% of defenders are still in man / zone behaviour 0.5 s after the handoff, and 22-24% of their pictures of the carrier are aged under the flag, the back behind the line). | `HEAD` | off | experimental (one head per player, perception proposal section 13, agreed with Coverage 2026-11-14) | engine, coverage | `src/sim/head.ts` (`want`, `wantAt`, `stepHeads`, `sideOf`), `src/sim/playsim.ts` (`sighted`'s view half) | set at first review. `Agent.head` for every player but the passer (his is QB_BODY_SEEN's until the QB lane moves it), recorded in the perception record each tick. His technique names one want a tick (`want(sim, a, target, reason)`); the head turns to it at GAZE's 6 rad/s within +-90 deg of body facing, relaxes to body facing with no want, and reaches a target behind him over the shoulder already turned to (Coverage's `sideOf`); `wantAt(a, t)` is what he was looking for at a past time. With SIGHT_COVER on, a coverage defender's view half is the cone (+-90 deg) of his recorded head at the sample's moment: seeing behind the shoulder is what his head allows. Alone it changes nothing (a game is identical: seed 101 hash 03e52d13bc18 off and on): the wants are written by Coverage in their techniques (coverMan, trackReceiver, zonePlay, Wall / Rob / Trap, RouteRunner's pre-release look, TrackBall), and BALL_EST moves its eye range onto `Agent.head` (their rebase). Read once the wants land. **Neck range (2026-11-14, with Coverage):** `neckRange(a)` = 90 deg standing narrowing linearly in his speed share to 45 deg at top speed, one function for every look (HEAD's heads and GAZE's clamp); 90 is anatomy, 45 and the linear shape are stated choices (sensitivity read at 60). A look past it is a body turn, priced by the facing kinematics. The SIGHT_COVER / HEAD controls are to be re-taken with it. |

| `HOLD_FINISH` | on | promoted 2026-11-10 (run-ref-11; `=0` restores); was experimental (unfinished holds, 2026-11-10) | defense | `src/sim/tackle.ts` (`updateBond`, `upperHoldHeight`); `src/physics/world.ts` (`graspHeight`) | set at first review. The holder's decision after the grasp forms. (1) Beside or behind the runner, he commits his weight against the runner's travel as hard as his grip lets him (request along the runner's path = speed × (1 − grip)), not only along his arms: beside the runner the arms' line is across his path, so the old plant asked for almost nothing and he ran along with his hands on the man. He commits only while his hold is not slipping (the joint's own slipping flag from last step, what his hands feel); while it slips he keeps his feet with the runner and sits again once it holds: a trailing man 5–7 yd/s slower than the runner otherwise sat while being ripped through his hands (firm-grip breaks with the grip load at capacity from the first planting tick). No timer; the World decides whether the grip survives the load. (2) An upper grip sits where his hands are, for the runner's current posture: the shoulder line (0.79 of stature, Flex's 1.60 yd) at leg share 0, joining the World's default (COM × (1 − 0.55 legs)) at the waist (0.5); below the waist the default stands. This replaces `HIP_BRACE`'s temporary chest-height default (removed from `graspHeight`). Flex's `scripts/diag/effective-mass/unfinished-holds.mts` (fixed 1,321 runs, run-ref-9): holds ≥ 0.3 s with the carrier at ≥ 4 yd/s 364 → 339 (running along 253 → 100, towed 111 → 239): he plants and is towed, because a rearward pull alone does not take the runner down (`HIP_BRACE` is the physics half). `npm run ref:report -- --flag HOLD_FINISH=1` on run-ref-9: all −0.06 (SE 0.03), broken a run 0.167 → 0.183, missed 0.229 → 0.235; of the extra broken holds, 16 had grips under 0.6 and 32 at or above (a firm grip that plants is also torn off). Package on run-ref-10 (with the slip rule): unfinished holds 349 → 310 with `HIP_BRACE` (271 with `GRASP_PATCH` too); broken holds a run (every hold, assists included) off 0.352, `HOLD_FINISH` 0.335, + `HIP_BRACE` 0.245; holds that re-plant 3+ times after running 171 / 206 / 86, ending in a run ≥ 0.5 s 287 / 286 / 177 (it converges to a sit or a break, not a jogging hold). Test `test/hold-finish.test.ts`. |

| `CUSHION_BREAK` | on | promoted 2026-11-12 (engine-36; `=0` restores); was experimental (defense, C9, 2026-11-11; candidate at engine-34) | defense | `src/sim/behaviors/defense.ts` (`cushionThreshold`, `cushionBroken`, `carrySpeedNeed`, `openTo`; inside zone calls only: the match-carry ManCoverage hip trigger, re-square, depth check and carry target, and the ZoneCoverage deep turn, re-square, depth check and carry target; pure man keeps its trigger) | A deep defender carrying a vertical in a zone call keeps the QB in view until the receiver breaks his cushion, and opens only as far as spending his cushion requires. Turn: the cushion left is less than the overlap he may give (both radii; positive with no deep help, negative with help) plus what the receiver closes during the rounded hip turn from his current facing (`roundedTurnTime`). Opening: the smallest angle off the line to the QB at which his facing cap toward the run gives the speed he needs; the need is the slowest speed from which he can still match the receiver by the time the cushion has shrunk to the turn threshold, at the acceleration his legs give (need = vx - sqrt(2 a (cushion - threshold))), so far over the top he stays square and spends cushion, and he comes back toward square when the receiver throttles. Carry target: a shoulder off the receiver's line (both radii plus 0.7 arm), one more reach with help as the receiver comes level. NFL (BDB 2025 weeks 1-2; nflcue.py, nflcarry.py): zone deep defenders over a receiver turn before the throw 8-10% with or without help; no-help defenders over a climber open 33 -> 62 deg from 1.5 to 3.0 s at 3.3 -> 4.9 yd/s, 0.9-1.3 yd off the receiver's line; side-on to running takes 0.40 s (ours 0.20). Engine-34 (two seeds x 16 leagueSample games, unpaired; off -> on, NFL): turned before the throw C2 53/59 -> 49/44%, C3 53/61 -> 40/40, C4 84/84 -> 30/29, C6 81/67 -> 40/43 (help/no help; 8-10); no-help carry angle 2 / 12 / 50 / 94 deg at 1.5 / 2.0 / 2.25 / 2.5 s (33 / 46 / 49 / 55); zone faster-receiver deep completion 29.5 -> 42.5% (52.6); deep share 7.4 -> 6.9% (7.5), completion 45.1 -> 49.2% (same operator NFL zone 49.3); zone contact before the throw 20.0 -> 20.1% (9.1); over the catch point at +0.2 / +0.4 s -0.85 / -0.23 -> -0.14 / +0.58 (+0.64 / +1.19); YAC trimmed 5.89 / 5.70 -> 5.19 / 5.06 (5.03); zone comebacks 85.8 -> 75.0% (64.6). Still turns at 2.5-2.75 s (33-51% no help): his cushion runs out about 0.5 s early, mostly from CUSHION_NEED pulling a deep carry forward at 0.25-0.75 s (follow-up), plus faster receiver releases (Coverage) and 0.4-0.6 yd shallower alignment. Test `test/cushion-break.test.ts`. |
