| `SHOULDER_READ` | on | promoted 2026-11-12 (engine-50; `=0` restores); was experimental (defense, C9 underneath arrival, 2026-11-12; repurposed by Coverage, C14, 2026-11-12: the underneath plant replaces the eyes-directed lean) | defense | `src/sim/behaviors/defense.ts` (`underneathPlant`, `stopDrop`; `ZoneCoverage.update` underneath seats, before the ball is out); `src/sim/playsim.ts` (`windupEndAt`); `src/sim/behaviors/qb.ts` (sets it on a pump or a pinned arm) | set at first review. An underneath zone defender who keys the QB as keysQB does (facing him, eyes held on him through his observation delay) stops his drop once he has seen the throwing motion: the part of his zone command taking him away from the line is dropped (he brakes it at his own rates); his lateral and downhill zone play and facing go on. No direction: the motion tells him when, not where. A pump is a windup: he plants on it and restarts only once he sees it pulled down, after his observation delay. Why: keysQB's cue is mature about 0.2 s before the release (observation delay p50 0.18 s vs windup 0.35-0.40 s) but `sawThrow` cannot act until the ball is out, and half these defenders are still dropping at the release. Attempt 1 (a full stop, squared to the QB) cancelled his movement toward threats as well and was reverted. Engine-47 seeds 11 / 23, 8 games each, off -> on: hook break p50 0.50 -> 0.35 / 0.50 -> 0.40 s, at or before the release 11 -> 14 / 10 -> 10%; curl/flat p50 0.40 -> 0.35 / 0.40 -> 0.30 s, 11 -> 15 / 13 -> 16%; toward the catcher at +0.3 s hook 0.81 -> 0.98 / 0.96 -> 1.02 yd/s, curl/flat 1.20 -> 1.20 / 1.00 -> 1.23 (NFL zone LB +2.4: most of the gap is direction, not the drop); wrong plants 0.07 / 0.12 per pump; commitment unchanged (turns 2%, depth flips 23 -> 22%). The eyes-directed lean is removed (as often wrong as right: QB's seen eyes on the catcher 35-45%). Review docs/reviews/2026-11-12-underneath-plant.md. Test `test/arrival.test.ts`. |

With No. 2 not (or no longer) carried vertically, the curl-flat defender who sees the back or No. 3 showing to the flat on his side (from 8 yd behind to 5 yd past the line, running outside at 1.5+ yd/s, 2+ yd outside the ball) sinks to his curl window and widens under it toward the flat with his eyes on the QB, and breaks on the throw (sawThrow / playBall). How far he widens follows what the QB shows him (his seen eyes, `seenQbEyes`): on the flat 0.55 + 0.3 play recognition of the way, on a receiver in his curl window 0.1, elsewhere 0.35 + 0.2 recognition once the back has crossed his face (0.15 before), plus up to 0.15 for range (top speed over 8 yd/s); weights estimated, stand-in debt. Why: on wide catches by the back against zone, nobody was in front of the catcher 50% vs NFL 12% (`outside_wide.py`, in_front.py's measure); the curl-flat owner was present on 42 of 44 of those calls but about 10 yd inside the catcher: his flat expansion covered only No. 2 and was switched off by a fast-releasing back (`wide-owner.ts`). Games on BACK_PATHS' tree (rules/back-paths 2404bba6 + this, BACK_PATHS=1, seeds 11 + 23 x 8, off -> on, NFL in brackets): the back's wide-flat YAC per catch p50 13.0 -> 6.8 (5.0), mean 16.7 -> 12.3 (5.9); behind him (non-back targets 5-12 yd, wide) completion 68 -> 66% (69), yd/target 9.95 -> 9.19 (6.23); RB wide nobody in front 39 -> 47% (12; n 61-70, unresolved). Attempt 1 (a fixed cheat, on develop): RB wide nobody in front 49 -> 31%, flat YAC p50 12.7 -> 3.7, behind him 68 -> 80% (the high-low opened). Behind him with the flag off the excess is YAC (air 6.8 vs NFL 7.3 yd, YAC per catch 7.8 vs 1.5), not this rule. Test `test/curl-flat-back.test.ts`. Diag `scripts/diag/pass-operators/outside_wide.py`, `flat_cost.py`, `scripts/diag/risk/wide-owner.ts`. |

Two halves. (1) A deep or quarter-field zone defender's landmark is never shallower than where he lined up (to the end line); calls that send a safety down give him an underneath zone and are unchanged. (2) A deep safety (aligned 9+ yd) stays deeper than the deepest route he sees in his band, by 13.4 - 0.5 x that route's depth (the NFL deep safeties' median: 13.0 / 11.7 / 8.5 yd over a route at 1.2 / 3.7 / 9.7 yd, BDB 2025, `safety_cushion.py`), scaled 9.6 / his top speed (0.85-1.25) x (1.1 - 0.2 play recognition) (estimated, stand-in debt), never under 2 yd, with his landmark as a floor; in his deep read (no vertical) the same rule over the deepest receiver in his band, the QB's eyes shading him laterally only; a route that stops climbing (a dig, a curl) no longer pulls him deeper: he holds the depth he had when he first saw it stop. Why: 21% of our safeties came downhill in the first second vs NFL 12% (Cover 4 38 vs 6%), 91-95% of them walking down to a landmark shallower than their alignment; and our deep safeties stopped at their 15-16 yd landmark by 1.5 s (deep-read, commanded 1.0 yd/s at 0.5 s), at the throw 15.8 vs NFL 18.3 yd, 6.2 vs 8.5 over the deepest route. The body achieves 0.5 / 1.1 / 2.8 of a commanded 1.4 / 3.7 / 5.0 yd/s backpedal at 0.5 / 1.0 / 1.5 s (backpedal start: Flex). Test `test/safety-depth.test.ts`. NFL envelope (BDB 2025 weeks 1-2, nflgait.py, dropbacks snap to release, p95 speed by the angle between body and travel, as a fraction of his own pre-throw top forward speed): CB 0.97 / 0.74 / 0.56 forward / side-on / backpedal; S 0.96 / 0.83 / 0.71 (deep 10+ yd 0.87 / 0.71, so not deep drift misbinned); LB 0.96 / 0.73 / 0.58. Why the ratio, not the tracked fraction of forward speed: the tracked fractions are of each player's pre-throw top speed (CB 8.0, S 7.5, LB 7.0 yd/s), not his true top speed (our vmax, CB about 9.8); within a player the ratio cancels that denominator, and in yd/s it lands on the tracked pedal (CB 4.9 vs NFL p95 4.5, S 5.1 vs 5.3, LB 4.4 vs 4.1), where the direct fraction overshot (CB 5.8 yd/s) and flattened every S and LB against his side-on cap. Controls (engine-30, seed 101, 16 games, vs off): OL depth / speed at 1.0 and 1.5 s identical (1.78 / 2.00, 2.73 / 2.46-2.47); runs 10+ 8.1 -> 10.8% (NFL 11.7; about two SE, plausibly LBs slower to recover depth in their fits; for the fixed run report); LB depth at 1.5 s 3.50 -> 3.45 yd (NFL 2.31: our drops are too deep for a reason other than the pedal, logged separately). Caveat: chosen speeds, envelope by p95, relative to pre-throw top speed; the shape is the evidence, the level is estimated. A shared capacity moved on direct measurement; not to be moved again to rescue a coverage number. Test `test/pedal-cap.test.ts`. |

Timed share of DL 75 -> 79%, timers' go p50 0.082 -> 0.046 s; defensive pre-snap fouls 1.75 -> 1.60% of snaps (2,000 snaps, snap-count test setup). Test `test/dl-snap-time.test.ts`. |

Each picture's position uncertainty is what the body could have done since he saw it, from its own acceleration (A = vmax / tau) over the age t: a defender up to min(A t^2/2, vmax t), his receiver (installed route, can only fall off its tempo) up to min(A t^2/2, v t) at his last seen speed. In time, through the defender's top speed, it widens the margin spreads in quadrature (contest 0.13 s, undercut 0.10 s; receiver term at the catch point only). Off, an aged picture is extrapolated and scored as if certain. throw-path.mts, run-ref-19: hot/panic throws 83, 78% hit before release, 89% to a receiver he was not reading, complete 53% |
| `PERIPH_ACUITY` | on | promoted 2026-11-23 (engine-92; `=0` restores); was experimental (QB picture leak, 2026-11-23; docs/reviews/2026-11-23-turned-down-open.md) | quarterback | `src/sim/qbmind.ts` (`periphSeen`, `pictureSpread`, the clear cone), `src/sim/receiver-forecast.ts` (`lastPicture`), `src/sim/behaviors/qb.ts` (`window`) | what he sees off his gaze is less exact. Position error grows with eccentricity (0.06 x range x eccentricity in rad, Levi, Klein & Aitsebaomo 1985) and velocity error with it (0.14 x speed x eccentricity, McKee & Nakayama 1984), scaled 1.3-0.6 x vision; the error is a stable draw per player (his misreading, not frame noise). The clear cone that resolves separation narrows to 10-15 deg half-width (Ball et al. 1988 useful field), the periphery stays as before. No must-look rule: the window prices the error (margin spreads and the accuracy sigma widen in quadrature), so a picture from the corner of his eye earns less confidence and an aged one less still. Off, a peripheral picture is exact apart from its delay: at decisions with the target 30 deg+ off his head the receiver error was 0.13-0.32 yd p50 |

On his own picture: before any lineman has shown him a set he takes his lane's first step (the finesse lane point); his mark is the nearest offensive lineman he sees in front of him on his way to the QB, inside 4 yd and within a body of that line, setting (seen moving back off the line faster than 0.3 yd/s) or already within 1.5 yd; aimed 0.3 s ahead of the man's seen motion; kept until he is level with him on the way to the QB (beaten: then the QB) or the man leaves his way. With the launch point, a back released from an under-centre play-action fake set beside the final launch point while the QB was still dropping, and the QB stopped behind him: staged under-centre PA (pa_post_dig / pa_shot / pa_flood x ace_11 / ace_12 / i_pro_21 / ace_trips_11 x cover 1-4 x rosters 1-6 x seeds 1-2, 576 plays), QB short of 6 yd at 2.0 s 21 -> 0, deeper by more than 0.5 yd on 126, shallower on none (p50 7.55 -> 7.65 yd); pa-drop's SET_SPOT pin removed. Default seed-999 hash 070dde7bbeb224de -> eae520a95142c574. |

| `EDGE_SET` | on | promoted 2026-11-16 (engine-73; `=0` restores); was experimental (Defense, 2026-11-16; Integration item 1, the end's width on a reach) | defense (edge rusher key read) | `src/sim/behaviors/defense.ts` (`PassRusher.decide`, the reach answer) | set at first review. An edge answering a reach sets the edge as taught (hands on the reach blocker's outside half, square, widen with him and no faster, squeeze the lane he is trying to open): his lateral target is the blocker's outside shoulder in his picture one beat ahead, fed forward at the blocker's lateral speed with no lead; wider, he works back to it; caught inside, he fights to the same shoulder (one continuous target, no side switch). Real outside zone toward the end (pa-backside-trace.mts PAB_CONCEPTS=oz_weak): widening 2.7 / 3.4 / 4.6 -> 0.9 / 2.2 / 3.3 yd at 1.0 / 1.5 / 2.0 s (NFL play-side end 0.4-0.6 / 1.3-1.6 / 2.3-2.8), within 1.5 yd of an OL 13 / 63 / 99% -> 100%; runs outside him 1/59 -> 3/63, none of the 10+ runs. PA grid (PA_HINGE, END_KEY=2): fake-side end unblocked 105 -> 36 of 576, PA sacks 19 -> 10 (docs/reviews/2026-11-16-edge-set.md). |
| `END_KEY` | 2 (numeric; default since engine-73, 2026-11-16; `=0` restores); was 0 (numeric bitmask) | experimental (Defense, 2026-11-16; Integration, the end's half of the PA backside end, attempt 1) | defense (edge rusher key read) | `src/sim/behaviors/defense.ts` (`PassRusher.decide`, `END_KEY_THROUGH`) | set at first review. Bit 2 (key through the triangle): an edge rusher whose keyed surface man releases (no blocking stance in his picture) keys on through to the tackle inside; the existing key reads then play the tackle's step (hinge / zone step away: squeeze or END_CHASE with the boot check; pass set: the rush). Sourced: Gridiron Strategies, "Defensive End Reads in a Forty Front" ("once you read release, look for the next part of triangle"). On PA_HINGE's staged PA grid the away-side end (DE_S) goes 0.58 / 1.85 / 2.80 -> -0.02 / 0.78 / 2.13 yd past the line at 1.0 / 1.5 / 2.0 s and squeezes 2.8 -> 1.4 yd outside his tackle (NFL away-side 0.25 / 1.32 / 2.06, squeeze 0.75 / 1.30 / 1.79). Bit 1 (the key's stance as a run/pass cue) was tried and removed: on PA it made the fake-side end commit run and fit inside (docs/reviews/2026-11-16-end-key.md). |
| `PA_HINGE` | on | promoted 2026-11-16 (engine-73; `=0` restores); was experimental (Flex, 2026-11-16; Integration, the offence's half of the PA backside end) | protection / play action | `src/sim/pa-look.ts` (`paHingeSpot`, `paFakeSide`, `PA_FAKE_TIME`), `src/sim/behaviors/blocking.ts` (`PassProtector.update`), `src/sim/assign.ts` (the fake's side from the call) | set at first review. On a zone run fake the line shows the zone it fakes: "all the offensive linemen step and reach play-side ... and have the backside offensive tackle or tight-end (whoever is the end man on the backside of your offensive line) hinge" (Youth Football Online, pass protection fundamentals; page). Until the fake's end (the back's mesh: called-drop.ts plannedFakeEnd, shared with the back and the QB; 0.7 s unless QB_FOOTWORK bit 16) every lineman takes two set steps (0.45 yd) to the fake's side on the line and the backside end man hinges (a step to the fake's side, two back); then each converts to his protection, one switch at the fake's end; a man who meets his rusher on the way engages. Gun and under centre alike. Sourcing: the instruction (reach play-side, the backside end man hinges) is a checked page; the hinge's footwork is not: pages describe the hinge only as the backside gate on gap / zone runs (step down to wall the inside gap, swing the outside leg back; Glazier OL training system, X&O Labs), and no page gives play-action hinge steps, so the steps here are that gate. The end's read of it is Defense's half (attempt 1 read: PA sacks 75 -> 30 on the QB lane's grid; the fake-side end's depth 0.01 / 0.76 / 1.87 yd at 1.0 / 1.5 / 2.0 s; cost: he widens about 2 yd with the reach where the NFL's holds his width, so his nearest OL is 3.0 / 3.1 / 2.1 yd against the NFL's 1.8 / 1.1 / 1.0, and he comes late, unblocked before the ball on 89 of 576, pa-hinge-pickup.mts; the away-side end runs near the dropback path; both are the ends' reads, Defense). NFL gun PA ends (nfl_pa_backside.py) past the line at 1.0 / 1.5 / 2.0 s: fake side 0.36 / 1.45 / 2.43, away side 0.25 / 1.32 / 2.06, against about 1.15 / 3.1 / 4.8 on dropbacks; ours (DE_W is the fake-side end in gun_doubles_11; the offset back fakes to his own side) ran the dropback path (pa-backside-trace.mts). |

| `LINE_START` | on | promoted 2026-11-12 (engine-42; `=0` restores); was experimental (C14 release, 2026-11-12; part A of two) | receivers | `src/sim/route-motion.ts` (`getOffDrive`, `firstBreakDepth`, `inGetOff`, `getOffLoss`, break approach in `steerRoute`), `src/sim/behaviors/receiver.ts` (`RouteRunner.getOff`, `breakTime`), `src/sim/receiver-forecast.ts`, `src/physics/kinematics.ts` (`MoveCommand.drive`, `arriveVelocity` decel) | set at first review. A receiver's first steps are a get-off, not a sprint start: until he declares his stem (his first step out of the stance and time to have read the defender, his observation delay) he pushes with a chosen share of his start force: 0.4 + 0.03 x the depth of his first break (to 10 yd), +-0.05 for a defender well off / in his face, +0.15 x (route running - 0.5), range 0.3-0.85 (estimated). Debt: the drive share is a fitted stand-in, adjusted twice against the NFL speed table and not to be adjusted again; it is replaced by the controller valuing lateral authority while he reads. What it stands in for: a full forward push spends the whole friction ellipse, leaving no lateral authority for those steps, and nothing in the controller yet values keeping it while he reads. `MoveCommand.drive` is that share (default 1: physics unchanged for every other caller; braking and cutting unscaled). Into a break he slows on his cutting ability (lateral x technique) rather than 0.8 of his braking. The quarterback's forecast of him and his expected break time use the same get-off. Games (sim-export seeds 11 + 23, 8 each, develop af9d7bd1, off -> on), speed at 0.5 s against the NFL (BDB 2025 weeks 1-3): off-coverage go 2.5 -> 1.8 (NFL 1.7), post / corner 2.5 -> 1.8 (2.0), in 2.5 -> 1.8 (1.8), out 2.3 -> 1.5 (1.8), hitch 2.2 -> 1.5 (1.7), slant 2.2 -> 1.2 (1.5), cross 2.3 -> 1.3 (1.8), flat 2.3 -> 1.4 (1.3); at 0.8 / 1.0 s the go 3.4 / 4.3 (NFL 3.4 / 4.4); depth at 1.5 s go 5.0 -> 4.4 (4.1), slant 4.4 -> 3.4 (3.5). Time to 5 yd +0.1 (go) to +0.2 s (slant, out, cross, flat). Pressed go vs man level with the corner 1.6 -> 1.9 s (NFL 2.2), depth at 3.0 s 13.8 -> 12.5 (11.6). Lane: bodies touching within 5 yd 17 -> 15%, around 22 -> 23%. Sacks (no-throw losses) 4.6 -> 3.8%, time to throw p50 2.45 -> 2.55 s. Openness table unchanged (tight / mid / open 49 / 59 / 70 -> 51 / 58 / 69%). Test `test/line-start.test.ts`. |
| `RELEASE_SEEN` | on | promoted 2026-11-12 (engine-45; `=0` restores); was experimental (C14 release, 2026-11-12) | receivers | `src/sim/behaviors/receiver.ts` (`RouteRunner.handleRelease` release-around, `tightAtSnap`) | set at first review. Model-rule repair: the release-around (PRESS_MIRROR / PRESS_RELEASE) was keyed on the defence's call (`defAlign.spots[d.slot].press && manTarget === a.slot`), a private assignment the receiver cannot know. With it he releases around any defender he saw tight over him at the snap (within 2.5 yd ahead and 1.5 yd either side, as he saw them), on that defender's seen position; release side and how cleanly he clears are the existing technique (route side vs seen shade; cut skill by release rating). The corner's own jam still reads the corner's assignment. Games (seed 11, 8 per arm, off -> on): stalled at the line (press_stall.py) 10 -> 7% (NFL 3%), with a lineman nearest 31 -> 12% (NFL 5%); off-ball tight receivers stalled 21 -> 13% (NFL 5%); press rows within 0.1 s to 5 yd. Review `docs/reviews/2026-11-12-release-seen.md`. Test `test/release-seen.test.ts`. |
| `HANDS_CUE` | on | promoted 2026-11-12 (engine-47; `=0` restores); was experimental (C14 man trail, 2026-11-12) | receivers, coverage | `src/sim/hands-cue.ts`, `src/sim/behaviors/receiver.ts` (`TrackBall.update`), `src/sim/behaviors/defense.ts` (`readsHandsAt`) | set at first review. A receiver's hands coming up for the ball as evidence a trailer within 3 yd can see. While he tracks a ball to him his hands come up a lead before it reaches his spot, 0.55 - 0.35 x route running s (stand-in debt: hands are not in tracking, so the lead is estimated, bounded by the trailer's observable break: recognition through the cue lands at p50 0.10 s after the secure event, later than the NFL trailer's break onset, p50 0.40 s before arrival, trail_break.py; late hands is receiver craft against this read); his running facing and speed are untouched (no separate head/hands channel exists, so it is a state, not a pose). The trailer's throw cue takes the earliest of his own sight of the ball, the existing look-back facing cue, and this, with his usual reaction delay. No hands without a ball coming (decoy hands not modelled: false-cue rate 0 by construction). Fixture (man-trail-fixture.ts, engine-44, off -> on): trailers who recognise the throw by the secure event 48/62 -> 56/60; those with no flight picture by then 14 -> 4 (off, 11 of the 14 recognised it at p50 0.20 s after); closing at +0.25 s pooled about 0.64 -> 0.71 yd/s (a trailer behind a faster crosser still cannot close). Test `test/hands-cue.test.ts`. |
| `CATCH_CHOICE` | on | promoted 2026-11-12 (engine-47; `=0` restores); was experimental (C14 catch, 2026-11-12) | receivers | `src/sim/catch-choice.ts` (`chooseCatchPoint`), `src/sim/behaviors/receiver.ts` (`TrackBall.update`) | set at first review. Where on the ball's path he plays it, as his choice: the reachable segment of the path from the earliest point (today's `catchSpot`: the high point, or coming back to a short ball) to the latest point with the ball still at or above his shoulders (0.82 of his height, estimated: hands carried while running); he plays the latest point the two nearest defenders, as he sees them, cannot reach before the ball by 0.1 s, else the point with the best margin over them. One judgment error per throw on those margins, sd 0.1 + 0.2 x (1 - ball tracking) s. Re-read every 0.1 s, committed in the last 0.3 s. Superseded and replaced `IN_STRIDE_CATCH` (removed 2026-11-12; see PARKED.md). Pooled read (seeds 11 + 23, 16 games each, on 3081064c; off -> on, NFL in brackets): deep man 0-2 yd completion 44 -> 58% (32), catch at 5+ yd/s with room short 93 -> 96%, deep 89 -> 86%; with CATCH_SPEED 87 / 80% (88 / 72); intermediate zone yd/att 9.11 -> 9.22 (6.85). Blocked in games by natural deep throws landing short of his full-speed spot (Rules+Coaching) and by the catch at speed (CATCH_SPEED). Review docs/reviews/2026-11-12-catch-choice.md. Test `test/catch-choice.test.ts`. |
| `CATCH_SPEED` | on | promoted 2026-11-12 (engine-47; `=0` restores); was experimental (C14 catch, 2026-11-12) | receivers | `src/sim/catching.ts` (`runTrackTerm`, the `shoulder` term) | set at first review. Tracking a ball he is not facing while he runs: replaces the over-the-shoulder step (from behind, above 6.5 yd/s only, +0.12-0.24) with K x clamp((speed - 3) / 4.5, 0, 1) x angle x clamp(air time / 1.3, 0, 1) x (0.7 + 0.6 x (1 - ball tracking)), angle from behind 1, straight across 0.46 (second constant, from the NFL's pooled across-vs-behind loss, 8.4 vs 18.4 points), facing past 120 deg 0; through hands like the other terms. By design it is smaller than the old step for a fast catch on a short-flight ball (air time well under 1.3 s: e.g. 0.15 at 0.6 s against the old 0.12-0.24 from behind above 6.5 yd/s), and larger on every deep ball (air time >= 1.3 s), so it can only make the same deep catch harder. K = 0.37, estimated from the NFL's fall in completion on well-placed balls with no defender within 2 yd (catch_speed.py: short 88%, deep 72% at 5+ yd/s; ours were 92-100 / 89-90). Debt: a stand-in for hands and eyes at speed; a physical model of the hand path against the ball replaces it. Pooled read (catch_speed.py, seeds 11 + 23, 16 games each, on 3081064c; baseline -> CATCH_SPEED -> with CATCH_CHOICE, NFL in brackets): at 5+ yd/s with no defender within 2 yd short 93 -> 95 -> 87% (88), deep 89 -> 84 -> 80% (72); defender within 2 yd short 73 -> 66 -> 65% (57), deep 65 -> 61 -> 53% (52); deep from behind 0-5 / 5-6.5 / 6.5+ 75 / 73 / 81 -> with both 84 / 66 / 68% (78 / 57 / 60); deep across his line stays easy (both 95 / 94 / 86% vs 65 / 53 / 73, n 7-96); control (short, facing it) 86 -> 85 -> 90% (77; the term is zero there). Deep man 0-2 yd completion 44 -> 53 -> 59% (32; n 81-98, ~5 pts sampling error). Forced outside go balls (identical throws by construction, forced-meet.ts, n 211): completions 110 -> 102 with CATCH_SPEED, 123 with CATCH_CHOICE, 115 with both. Test `test/catch-speed.test.ts`. |

| `TKL_WHIFF` | off | experimental | tackling (TKL-7, open-field slice, with Flex) | `src/sim/tackle.ts` (`whiffs`) | set at first review. An open-field reach or dive misses when the carrier's body is not where the tackler's hands can get to in the reach window (0.2 s at full extension, 0.3 s for a dive, shorter the less the arms must extend): the tackler's centre coasts on his momentum, redirected toward his delayed picture only by his cut capacity; the carrier, if he sees him, cuts away by his own. Replaces the rating roll; pocket QBs keep their escape law. Not for promotion: on 324 matched catches YAC 10.47 -> 11.55 (misses 16 -> 68, broken 52 -> 8). The physics misses reaches the roll was saving: 68% of after-catch reaches come from behind and half need at least half of full extension (miss 41% there, 0% below). The lever is the approach (pursuit angles that put the tackler in front) and the hold, not the whiff decision. On runs it goes the other way: `ref:report` (1,321 runs) missed 0.241 -> 0.188 and broken 0.175 -> 0.124 a run, paired +0.04 (SE 0.07), 10+ 9.3 -> 9.4%: the roll misses too much front-on and too little from behind. Quick scorecard: YAC 10.53 -> 11.67, sacks 12.1 -> 9.0% (the dive branch has no pocket exception) |
| `OPEN_CARRY` | on | promoted 2026-11-12 (engine-44; `=0` restores); was candidate (engine-43 tree, with LINE_START on) | defense (C9, eyes in on the match carry) | `src/sim/behaviors/defense.ts` (`openCarryFacing`; man-open, carry-run-past, lane-carry), `src/sim/coverage/techniques.ts` (`Wall`), `src/sim/coverage/defender.ts` (`zoneEyes`) | set at first review. An underneath zone or match defender carrying a route keeps his hips as square to the QB as his run allows: the CUSHION_BREAK `openTo` rule (smallest opening off the QB, toward the receiver, at which his body's facing speed cap reaches the speed his own carry request asks for), so poorer hips or speed open further, sooner. On a vertical, a deep teammate over the receiver in his own picture (3+ yd deeper, within 12 yd laterally) caps him at side-on: he gives ground and the route goes to the help; with no help in his picture he opens as far as the speed needs. An underneath match-man job inside a zone call keeps zone eyes (he keys the throwing motion when his facing lets him see it). Pure man and deep carries unchanged. Why (BDB 2025 weeks 1-2 vs sim, nfleyes.py / eyesprobe.mts by nearest receiver): zone defenders running with a route from 3-6 yd face the QB within 90 deg 91% at release - 0.3 s, ours 59-62%. Attempt 1 (facing only) squared them (3-6 yd with a receiver 58 -> 78%) but the break did not move: a match carry ran as a ManCoverage sub, which read his man, not the QB. Attempt 2 adds zone eyes. Engine-41 + #621, 16 games x seeds 101/201: facing the QB within 90 deg DB 0-5 54 -> 82%, LB 5-10 69 -> 85% (NFL 86-94); keyed the throwing motion 2 -> 78% of underneath match carriers holding at the throw; distance to his man while the ball is held unchanged to 1.5 s (over 5 yd at 2.0 s 22 -> 29%, the side-on speed cost); yd/dropback 9.22 -> 8.63, completion 65.2 -> 62.1%; zone verticals 54.7% / 15.45 -> 48.1% / 13.64 yd/att; speed toward the catch at +0.4 s, flights 0.6-1.0 s, 0.57 -> 0.94 (never broke 47 -> 41%); never reached 14.0 -> 13.2%. Known costs: zone crossers completed 65.2 -> 71.1% (yd/att 10.36 -> 9.81); slower breaks under the catch point (+1.88 -> +1.56 at +0.4 s) and on flights over 1.0 s (+1.10 -> +0.75). Next (out of scope): passing a route off (lost man 27% vs NFL 14%, pass-off 10-12% vs 18%). |
| `CATCH_CARRY` | on | promoted 2026-11-12 (engine-47; `=0` restores); was experimental | defense (P1, short-catch YAC: the catch-to-tackle conversion) | `src/sim/behaviors/defense.ts` (`pursueIfLive`, `catchDrive`) | set at first review. A defender on a receiver the ball reached (he drove on the catch, or his own flight picture had it arriving at that man; within 5 yd of him as he sees him) keeps driving on that receiver, as he last saw him, through the hands, until his own picture resolves the outcome: a holder (pursuit, as before), a newer picture of the receiver without the ball (dropped), or the ball back in the air (broken up). No earlier knowledge of the catch than observedHolder gives. Continuity only. Why (settled catches 3-10 yd against zone, nearest non-DL within 3.5 yd; engine-42, settletrace.mts; NFL from settlecurve.py): from the catch until his picture of it (0.15-0.35 s) he went to the ball's acquired landing area (pursueIfLive's unresolved-ball branch, 105 of 132 ticks at +0.3-0.5 s), a fixed spot the catcher was leaving; his request turned 99 deg off his run and his speed toward the catcher fell 3.5 -> 2.0 yd/s as the catcher started to move (NFL +3.5 at +0.4 s). |

| `SETTLE_DEPTH` | on | promoted 2026-11-16 (engine-73; `=0` restores); was experimental (C14 underneath layer / task 126 UNDER_PASS part ii, 2026-11-15; `=1` turns on; docs/reviews/2026-11-15-underneath-handoff-design.md) | defense | `src/sim/behaviors/defense.ts` (`ZoneCoverage.shortOfDepth`, the ZONE_SETTLE caps in `update` and `spotUnderneath`) | set at first review. ZONE_SETTLE plants an underneath dropper at the depth he has when he sees the QB set: right for a man who reached his drop, wrong for one a route pulled down (a hook who walled or carried the shallow settled at 5-6 yd against a 10 yd landmark, under the dig). On, a dropper short of his landmark's depth by more than 2 yd keeps gaining depth toward it while he reads the QB, until within 1 yd (stated margins, enter and exit on opposite sides); one at depth settles as before. |
| `ZONE_HOLD` | 114 (numeric; default since engine-73, 2026-11-16; `=0` restores); was 0 (numeric bitmask) | experimental (C14 underneath layer / commit rule, 2026-11-16; docs/reviews/2026-11-16-zone-hold.md) | defense (zone coverage) | `src/sim/behaviors/defense.ts` (ZoneCoverage threat band and the deep vertical test; `seenQbEyes`) | set at first review. Bit 1 (band): a receiver joins a zone defender's threat set when his pictured future is inside the zone's lateral band by 0.5 yd and leaves only when outside it by 1.5 yd; the deep zones' vertical test has the same pair (in past 7.5 yd or climbing over 1.5 yd/s, out inside 6.5 yd and under 0.5 yd/s). Bit 2 (eyes): whom he reads the QB looking at is kept until another receiver's bearing beats it by 4 deg or the QB's pictured head turns clear of the current cone by 6 deg. Bit 16 (pick): the underneath lane pick keeps the receiver he is playing until another's lane score beats it by 1.5 yd. Bit 4 halves the margins, bit 8 doubles them (margins read at three levels; stated choices). Measured: bits 2+16 (=18) halve eyes reversals and cut the flat target's reversals 0.27 -> 0.08 at no delay to a real switch; bit 1 adds ~0.2 s before a leaving receiver is dropped for little gain; underneath set comp 0.703 -> 0.705, yd/throw 10.35 -> 10.44. Without them both were re-picked every tick on a knife edge (exact pictures, zone-flips.mts: eyes' target changing 1.6-2.0/s, a tenth reversing inside 0.3 s; the flat defender reversing 27% of his target changes). Bit 32 (lean, 2026-11-16): the settled underneath defender's lean with the QB's eyes slides along the QB's seen head line (the point at his landmark's distance), held on a 1 yd leash, not toward whichever receiver the eyes are on. Bit 64 (hand-off from motion): when a seam carry ends (route breaks down in his picture or reaches the hand-off depth) or a lane read gives way to the settle, the new target may move anywhere across his motion but not back against it short of his own stopping point; continuous in his speed, no threshold or timer. Measured (zone-settle trace, docs/reviews/2026-11-16-zone-hold.md): request reversals in the last 0.5 s 0.36 -> 0.32 (18 -> 114), carry-seam->lane reversals 37 -> 5, closers at the throw 0.44 -> 0.50 (cue_onset), underneath set comp/yd 0.773/10.27 -> 0.770/10.30; bit 32 waits on the QB's head turning at its rate (QB lane). |

| `FIT_TE_GAP` | on | promoted 2026-11-10 (run-ref-17; `=0` restores); was experimental (run fits, ported from #396, 2026-11-10) | defense | `src/playbook/alignment.ts` (`techniqueGap`, `alignDefense`), `src/playbook/defense.ts` (Over Sam), `src/sim/behaviors/defense.ts` (`PassRusher` lane), `src/sim/assign.ts` | set at first review. A 6 or 7 technique on an attached end owns C, the gap inside him (Browns OL manual p. 11), not D beside the 9; the 4-3 Over's Sam outside him (a "90") takes D; the end still rushes outside the TE (`DefSpot.rushGap`), so his pass lane is apart from his run gap and contain is kept. Off, C between the tackle and the TE has no owner at the snap. Gap accounting (`scripts/diag/align/gap-accounting.mts`, 24,216 alignments): strong C holes 6,379 -> 4,040; Ace 11 vs Over strong C unowned 114 -> 0 of 144; unowned gaps 36,917 -> 36,264; duplicates 5,699 -> 5,046; fully sound 2,082 -> 2,332. Fixed 1,321 runs (`ref:report`, run-ref-15): +0.05 +/- 0.05 yd a run. Test `test/fit-te-gap-slide.test.ts`. |
| `FIT_BLITZ_SLIDE` | on | promoted 2026-11-10 (run-ref-17; `=0` restores); was experimental (run fits, ported from #396/#398, 2026-11-10) | defense | `src/sim/run-fits.ts` (`buildRunFits`) | set at first review. A called blitzer takes his gap and the lineman whose gap he takes moves at most one gap, to the nearest open neighbour (a nose under a Mike blitzing strong A plays weak A); with neither neighbour open (double A over a nose) he stays as a two-gap player (technique `control`). His pass-rush lane is the same gap. Defense's decision on #396 (2026-11-02). Off, the blitzer and the lineman both own the gap and its neighbour has none. Fixed 1,321 runs: +0.01 +/- 0.04 yd a run (it acts only against called blitzes). Test `test/fit-te-gap-slide.test.ts`. |
| `CALL_MIX` | on | promoted 2026-11-11 (run-ref-20; `=0` restores); was experimental (play calling, 2026-11-11) | play calling | `src/game/call-mix.ts`, `src/game/playcaller.ts` (`choosePass`) | the coordinator prefers route classes by down and distance. Each pool concept's weight is multiplied by prod over classes of pref[bucket][class] ^ (the concept's share of installed routes in that class). Classes: quick (hitch/stick/hook, quick out, short in, slant, under 8 yd), flat (flat, arrow, back routes), cross, breaking (curl, comeback, out, deep out, dig), vertical. Seven buckets (1st; 2nd and 3rd/4th short 1-3, medium 4-7, long 8+). The table is `config/call-mix.json`, fitted by `scripts/diag/call-mix/fit.py` so the called routes-run class mix matches BDB 2025 weeks 1-3 dropbacks (route label plus break depth from tracking, `nfl_route_depth.py`); SCREEN is left out as the named remainder. `test/call-mix.test.ts` keeps `CALL_MIX_PREF` equal to the config. Evidence: docs/reviews/2026-11-11-call-mix.md. Card (league-sampled, seeds 101-116 + 201-216, unpaired, 2,970 vs 3,015 dropbacks): breaking routes run 17.6% -> 15.6% (NFL 13.1%), quick 22.7 -> 24.9 (26.7); breaking targets 26.4% -> 24.2% (NFL 11.5%); yards an attempt 10.28 -> 10.22, completion 62.0 -> 62.6%, sacks 2.4 -> 2.3%, INT 4.1 -> 3.4% (all within noise). Moves the call as fitted; too small to show in yards. |

Adds 15 drop-back concepts to the caller's pool, built from the NFL route combinations our 17 concepts never produced (docs/reviews/2026-11-11-playbook-coverage.md, BDB `routeRan` on drop-back passes): each strong side's #1-#2 is a missing pair (double slants, fade-flat, hitch-post, double outs, hitch-out, seam-slant, hitch-cross, post-cross, corner-out, corner-flat) and its #3 a missing trips side (hitch-hitch-flat, go-out-hitch, hitch-seam-hitch, go-out-quick out, go-seam-hitch); the back runs angle, out (new `back_out` route) or wheel. Each carries a plain-text note of its intended key and rule (`read`) with `progression` ordered to match; the QB runs the progression. CALL_MIX weights them from their route classes. With `BACK_RELEASE` a concept's named back route is what the released back runs. Test `test/concept-menu.test.ts`. |

On: a pursuit of a carrier he has seen (pursueIfLive's chase of an observed holder, CATCH_CARRY's drive on the catcher) is not contained; drops, carries, catch anticipation, flight continuation and the rally to the ball's area stay contained. |
| `PURSUIT_COMMIT` | on | promoted 2026-11-13 (engine-58; `=0` restores); was experimental | defense (open-field pursuit, commitment rule) | `src/sim/behaviors/common.ts` (`commitMeeting` in `pursuitPoint`), `src/sim/behaviors/defense.ts` (`playObservedFlight`) | A pursuer keeps the meeting moment he chose on his current picture of the carrier until it is unreachable by 0.15 s (or a fallback becomes reachable with 0.1 s to spare), a fresh meeting is better by 0.3 s, it is under 0.15 s away or the target changes; before, the first reachable forecast sample was re-picked every tick, flipping between reachable and least-late fallback and jumping the meeting time on 9-13% of ticks, 35-40% of his aim-point turn (`scripts/diag/effective-mass/pursuit-weave.mts`). | `BALL_EST` | 6145 (numeric bitmask; default since engine-72, 2026-11-16; `=0` restores the true ball flight for every player) | promoted for receivers with BREAK_LOOK and SEEN_EMPTY (three seeds, completion about -1.4 pts with the landing set, cause stated: post-sample steering is a physical redirect and the catch model); defenders still read the true flight (bits 2049/8192 on the Coverage branch); was: 6145 (receivers on their own sight of the ball) carded clean alone at engine-70 and is held on an interaction with the route and spacing holds; experimental, parked designs 1-4 and 6 (C14 coverage, 2026-11-14) | coverage / receivers | `src/sim/ball-estimate.ts`, `src/sim/possession-read.ts` (`acquireFlight`), `src/sim/behaviors/receiver.ts` (TrackBall, RouteRunner pre-release look), `src/sim/behaviors/defense.ts` (`playBall`), `src/sim/catching.ts` (`trackPen`) | set at first review. Each player's own estimate of a ball in flight from the ball he has seen, replacing the defender's and the receiver's copies of the true path. Bits: 1 the estimator; 2 removes catching.ts's tracking penalty (double-counts ballTracking); 4 a committed catch point moves with his estimate; 8 looming (time to contact); 16 the deep receiver looks back from his landmark depth, and the neck range narrows with speed (design 6); 32 incidental catches and defenders play the ball only on their own sight of it (passive sight included, reaction-gated). With HEAD=1 his looks are wants on the shared head (head.ts: want "ball" or "qb") and the neck range is neckRange; with HEAD off BALL_EST keeps its own head. Spec and results: `docs/reviews/2026-11-14-ball-in-air-spec.md`. Bit 64 (design 7, with TRUNK_TWIST; attempt 1 of 2, 2026-11-14): his look (`lookFor`, `preLook`, TrackBall past 3 yd) goes through the trunk before the hips: head and neck (`neckOf`), then the shoulders off the hips for what the neck cannot reach (to TRUNK_RANGE), then the hips only for what is left; the target is his own estimate (or the QB before he has one), never the true ball. 16 games seed 101, HEAD=1, TRUNK_TWIST on, out of view (ball past neck + 30 deg off his shoulders) 7 / 71: ball 90-120 deg off his run 29 / 22%, 120-180 deg 52 / 39%, flat 26 / 7%, back 23 / 10%, deep 74 / 72% (23 / 87: deep 69 / 61%); speed while tracking 120+ deg 4.95 / 5.58 yd/s, drift off his release line 0.62 / 0.30 yd/s; catch rates within noise (deep 52 / 44%, 23 / 87: 46 / 50%, n about 200). Bit 128 (defense only, 2026-11-14): the estimator for the defense alone (`acquireFlight`, and with bit 32 his own-sight play on the ball); receivers keep today's tracking. Cards as 129 then 161. Witness scripts/diag/risk/def-ball.ts. Bit 1024 (2026-11-15, attempt 1 of 2): the receiver's feet run on his own image of the ball (`src/sim/ball-servo.ts`; design note `docs/reviews/2026-11-15-receiver-feet-servo.md`): his speed changes with the optical acceleration of tan(elevation) (OAC) and he accelerates across his line of sight with the drift of the ball's bearing (proportional navigation, N 3), fit by least squares over his last 1.0 s of samples (at least 8 over 0.5 s), on a held command seeded from his route, handing over with 0.35 s left. Witness scripts/diag/risk/deep-track.ts (forced deep probe). Bit 1024 is the receiver's one card meaning (the receiver on his own sight): it carries bits 2, 4, 16 and 64, so `BALL_EST=1025` equals 1111; the single receiver bits remain for the parked designs' reads. Bit 2048 (the glance rule, 2026-11-15, the user's direction via Integration): once the ball has been in his own eyes for a dwell (0.10 s for the intended target, 0.15 s for anyone else, x (1.5 - skill)), his belief of the flight is the flight plus one correlated error per look, `BALL_GLANCE_DEPTH`% of his range along his line of sight and 2% across, x (1.5 - skill), shrinking with time held as sqrt(0.3 / (0.3 + held)) and growing with time looked away as (1 + age / 0.5); no look, nothing (`test/ball-glance.test.ts`). It carries 2/4/16/64 and turns the servo off; card `BALL_EST=2049`. Bit 4096 (receivers only, 2026-11-15, the mirror of bit 128): the estimator for the offense alone, defenders keep today's read of the flight; the receiver-side card is `BALL_EST=6145`. Under 2049 the defense's side moves YAC per reception 7.50 -> 14.10 (seed 11 x 16, `scripts/diag/risk/yac-split.ts`; receivers only 6.74): a defender without his own estimate never acquires a flight (`acquireFlight` returns empty), so the HONEST_READS post-catch path never starts for him. The fit and the servo stay as the deeper version. Closing speed for its lateral law is read from looming (his range over the ball's optical time to contact, TAU_SD), not differenced range. |

Design 6 attempt 2 (parked): the head's useful range a side at his top speed (90 at a standstill, linear in his speed share); choice, sensitivity read at 60. The neck-range rule itself lives in HEAD (#720 has adopted it); this copy is BALL_EST's own head until the rebase onto #720, and must not drift from it |

| `BREAK_LOOK` | on | promoted 2026-11-16 (engine-72; `=0` restores); was experimental (C14 pass game, 2026-11-16; attempt 1; with BALL_EST, carded on the glance 6145) | coverage / receivers (look timing) | `src/sim/behaviors/receiver.ts` (`RouteRunner.preLook`) | set at first review. A deep-family route the plan throws on its break (`BREAK_DECL` throw family `onBreak`: corner, post, seam read) gets the break-timed pre-look as well as design 6's depth look (DEEP_LOOK_DEPTH 12 yd), whichever comes first; a go, fade or seam (`seenOpen`) is unchanged. Without it, on the paired staged throws (`scripts/diag/catch/paired-throws.ts`, engine-70, 305 throws), three smash corners broken short of 12 yd and thrown on the break never sampled the ball under the glance (ball 150-175 deg behind his hips at his body-turn window). His own route and break are the cue; no new information. Declared clear-outs (compile.ts `CLEAR_OUTS`) are all goes, so none is affected. No new constants. |
| `SEEN_EMPTY` | on | promoted 2026-11-16 (engine-72; `=0` restores); was experimental (C14 pass game, 2026-11-16; attempt 1; with BALL_EST, carded on the glance 6145) | coverage / receivers (throw cue) | `src/sim/ball-estimate.ts` (`BallEstimates.sense`, `sawEmpty`, `lookedAtRelease`), `src/sim/behaviors/receiver.ts` (`RouteRunner.preLook`) | set at first review. A receiver who sees the passer without the ball (his own delayed picture, the passer within his eyes' range) has the throw cue, as if he had seen the arm: TrackBall keeps his look on the ball's search after the release instead of waiting for the body-turn window. His pre-look for the passer ends when he sees the empty hand in his eyes, not on the passer's state through the field-wide picture (off: it ended with his head 90 deg off the passer) and not at the arm (which ended it 0.25 s before the ball left). Paired staged throws (engine-70, 6145, 305): catches 187 = 187 (1 won, 1 lost in reach), never sampled 38 -> 31, 15 throws sample earlier and none later; with BREAK_LOOK 30 -> 23. It says the ball is up, not where or whose; it is never the release time (the estimate's seed keeps the arm or his first sample). Read only by the targeted receiver's TrackBall: an untargeted receiver runs his route on as before (his pre-look ends on the empty hand) until he sees the catch. No new constants. |

Depth gain of the feet servo: yd/s^2 of acceleration per unit optical acceleration of tan(elevation) (1/s^2). Stated choice; no published gain maps to yd and s; sensitivity read at 7.5 and 30. |
| `BALL_SERVO_KB` | 30 (numeric, tenths) | experimental (with BALL_EST bit 1024) | coverage / receivers | `src/sim/ball-servo.ts` | set at first review. Navigation constant N of the feet servo's lateral law in tenths (30 = 3): acceleration across his line of sight = N x closing speed x bearing rate (proportional navigation; N > 2 converges, 3-5 in use). Sourced; read at 20 and 40. Was a heading-rate gain of 1.5, whose sign is wrong with the ball behind him (2026-11-15). |
| `BALL_EST_TAU_SD` | 7 (numeric, percent) | experimental (with BALL_EST bit 8) | coverage / receivers | `src/sim/ball-estimate.ts` | set at first review. Time-to-contact precision sigma_tau / tau, in percent, for BALL_EST's looming row (choice; TTC discrimination thresholds run about 5-15%); the sensitivity reads use 0.035 and 0.14. |
| `PURSUIT_SPOT` | on | promoted 2026-11-14 (engine-61; `=0` restores); was experimental (engine-61 candidate, defense, for TRACK_PURSUIT; intended on with it) | defense (open-field pursuit, commitment rule) | `src/sim/behaviors/common.ts` (`commitSpot` in `pursuitPoint`) | set at first review. PURSUIT_COMMIT's pursuer commits to the meeting spot he chose, not its moment. Under TRACK_PURSUIT the carrier is a tracked picture whose velocity the tracker corrects every tick; a meeting moment held fixed turned each correction into a shift of his aim point along the carrier's path, so on the engine-61 candidate the commit made pursuers turn more (pursuit-commit, 3-8 yd, request turn p50 off 11.2 -> on 16.3 deg/s; held-moment ticks 14.0 deg/s vs the re-solved intercept's 10.6). The held spot is released when the carrier's forecast path no longer passes it within reach (a cut), the pursuer can no longer reach it in time (MEET_EXIT), a fresh reachable meeting is earlier by MEET_MARGIN, or it is under MEET_NEAR away. Candidate with it on: 3-8 yd 11.2 -> 8.1, 0-3 yd 51.5 -> 34.0, 15+ 11.2 -> 7.9 deg/s; meeting-bearing jumps at 3-8 yd 13/1330 -> 15/1023. Without TRACK_PURSUIT it is worse than the moment commit (3-8 yd 15.1 vs 6.5): it belongs with the tracked picture. A braking carrier (7 -> 3 yd/s straight ahead, pursuer 5 yd off) releases the spot in steps about 0.1 s behind the re-solved intercept and is within 0.35 yd of it 0.35 s after the brake (Rules+Coaching's review case). Dither guard passes off and on (pursuit 0.400 -> 0.392 /s); fresh run set on the candidate -0.02 (SE 0.05) vs placebo mean +0.01 / RMS 0.05, 73% of plays changed. Tests `test/pursuit-commit.test.ts` (with PURSUIT_SPOT=1 on the candidate stack), `test/pursuit-spot.test.ts`. |

| `APEX_LEVERAGE` | 7 | promoted 2026-11-14 (engine-62; all bits on; `=0` restores); was experimental (C14 coverage, 2026-11-13) | coverage | `src/sim/coverage/rules.ts` (`apexWall`, `flatThreat`, `mikeWiden`), `src/sim/coverage/techniques.ts` (`Wall` options), `src/sim/coverage/defender.ts` | set at first review. The quarters / Cover 6 apex and Mike against No. 2's vertical and a flat back, from the NFL apex (BDB 2025 PFF CF, n 1720; `apex_leverage_nfl.py`). 1: the apex walls No. 2 level, not 0.8 yd inside. 2: once he commits to the back as a flat threat to his side (seen 1.5 yd outward at 1.5 yd/s; exits inside 0.5 yd or on an under / in / vertical declaration; the NFL's earliest outward tell is a flat-side release only 52-55% of the time, so no earlier commit), he stops leading No. 2 (the safety has him), widens to No. 2's outside and holds ~9 yd over the back he sees, never inside 1 yd of the line and never coming up to make it. 4: the quarters Mike widens to the hook on the back's side on a flat declaration (enter 2 yd outward, exit inside 1 yd; under / in / vertical return him to the back). The 1 yd outside, 9 yd cushion and the margins are measured or estimated stand-ins, not coached. Attempt 2 (seeds 11/23/37/51 x 8 games, unpaired; `scripts/diag/risk/apex-out.ts`, `mike-out.ts`, `apex-fixture.ts`), off -> 7 (NFL): apex depth at 2.5 s with a flat back 7.9-8.5 -> 6.0-6.7 (6.2); out vs No. 2 -0.7..-0.9 -> -0.3..+0.2 (+2.1); out vs the back -2.2..-3.8 -> -0.7..-2.6 (+0.6); flat-back sides: back targeted 47 / 101 at 7.2 yd -> 42 / 107 at 5.7, No. 2 9 at 10.6 -> 14 at 12.4; No. 2 vertical targeted 5 / 85 sides at 16.4 -> 6 / 77 at 8.6; middle throws 77 at 8.8 -> 92 at 7.9; staged seam-flat vs quarters (192 plays): No. 2 nearest defender at the throw 2.1 -> 3.0 yd, throws to him 12 -> 12 at 27.4 -> 29.6 yd; slip screen vs Cover 4: 6.8 -> 6.9 yd; draws: the Mike never widened. Open (KNOWN_PROBLEMS COV-11): he gets level with No. 2, not 2 yd outside; the seam is roomier in the staged fixture though not thrown more in games. Test `test/apex-leverage.test.ts`. |
